# Changelog / Refinement Log

> Moved out of `CLAUDE.md` on 2026-05-19 to keep project instructions lightweight. This is the chronological build log; it is not loaded into every session.

- 2026-09-22 (owner: "Sounds good. Let's build it"): **"When is this for?" is an editor setting.** The hover line (`when`) a teacher can write on any activity, including copies that live only in the database: a two-row box under Description in the designer's Settings panel (`#game-when`, 140 characters, helper copy in the teacher's words), rendered and read back by editor.js (blank = the field is dropped and the yard shows the description), validated as text-or-nothing in `engine/game-loader.js` with the client mirror, and carried through both AI edit sites (`carryWhen`, revise and chat) so a whole-config rewrite keeps the teacher's sentence. Guard: `tests/screens/when-setting.test.js`. `engine/when-lines.js` stays until the owner writes the line into Rose, Bud, Thorn on the live site; then its row can go.
- 2026-09-22 (owner's list on PR #63, branch `home-16h`, FOR VIEWING): **The home without the carousel, and the picture in three scenes.** (1) The "See one on the projector" carousel is gone (its section, CSS, and script); the painted code blocks, pile, and answer chips it owned moved into `shared/fold-picture.css`. (2) The 13a how-it-works strip is back, laid flat in one row above the yard (step one now says "Find one in the yard and make it yours"; "Pick an activity" is a phrase the 15b vocabulary test forbids). (3) The yard's head carries the carousel's old line beside its name: "Activities ready to be made to fit your class". (4) The headline breaks after "class" (a forced `<br>`, as the handoff breaks it) and the picture beside it gives way instead of the words (h1 `flex: 0 0 auto`, picture `flex: 0 1 auto` + zoom). (5) "What does your class need?" is spaced caps, as the handoff sets it. (6) The plank ways are back on hover, to compare with the always-on day. (7) The picture plays THREE scenes (twenty states, about fourteen seconds): Snowball's join and first answer; the pair's ONE shared box ("Maya + Jordan · one shared box", the partner's words arrive, a cyan "Jordan has the pen" tag turns yellow "Your turn", "We agree, submit" presses, "Now in pairs" on the projector, then "What the pairs built"); and the wall ("Draw your favorite animal", a doodle appears on the student's box, Submit, three drawings land on the projector wall with names, "3 of 3 on the wall"). Pieces that share a slot carry `data-until` and `data-hide` (display none until they arrive, a keyframe for the entrance). Doodles are three inline SVG symbols. (8) `engine/when-lines.js`: `when` lines for activities that live only in the database, keyed by id, filled in by the games list when a row has none; Guess Who: Rose, Bud, Thorn gets the owner's line ("When your class needs to check in on each other and have fun doing it. Great for advisory."); an editor field for `when` is still the real fix. (9) The AI door reads from the need side: name "Have an idea? Make it real", meta "describe it, then host it", print caps "Say what you need", and a hover card like the prints' ("When what your class needs isn't on this shelf yet. Say it in a sentence and it gets built while you watch."). Tests: `home-16h.test.js` rewritten for the scenes and the page, `when-lines-fallback.test.js` new, two carousel assertions retired (`review-open-items-2026-09-12`, `yard-paint`), `home-15b` updated (h1 break, hover ways, AI door name, no carousel door). Shots at 1366 (three moments, full page, plank hover, the AI door's card) and 1024 in the session scratchpad.
- 2026-09-21 (design handoff 16h, branch `home-16h` on top of `home-whole-class`, FOR VIEWING): **The fold's picture.** Beside the headline, a drawn projector print and one student's screen play Snowball's first minute once on load (`screens/shared/fold-picture.js` + `.css`, mounted from the home): the code lands on the projector, the student taps it in and joins (the header's join tab lifts for that beat), three names pile up in the room, the prompt arrives on both screens, the student types "One voice at a time" and presses Submit, three blocks land on the pile with a green "N of 3 in", and "What the pairs built" comes up with Snowball's three sample answers while the pile steps back. Thirteen states on the handoff's dwell table (about nine seconds), the system's one entrance move for every arrival, then the page is still; a Replay button under the picture runs it again (never a mouse sweep, which would restart it on the way to the join tab). Departures from the handoff, all owner-approved before the build: the story is Snowball's real shape (the pairs' list, not a MOST VOTES crown Snowball does not have), the student's button says Submit as the real one does (not ADD MY BLOCK), the plank ways stay on the planks, the canvas is a fixed 660 by 330 drawing scaled to its column with CSS zoom (the handoff fits only at 1366; the picture drops under the headline below 1100px and a phone gets the finished state, still), and the pieces are the home's own (`.code-row .cb-N`, `.pile-block`, `.pile-plinth`, `.pile-base`, `.frame-answers`). The sub-line and the mechanic line are gone (the picture does that job); a visually hidden sentence says it for screen readers, the canvas is `aria-hidden` and unselectable, and the replay button sits outside it. Reduced motion renders the finished state. The markup carries the finished picture, so with no script it is simply done. The "See one on the projector" carousel below the yard stays for the owner to judge with both on the page. Guard: `tests/screens/home-16h.test.js` (the pure state table, Snowball's words, the page wiring). Shots at 1366, 1280, 1024, 600 and mid-sequence in the session scratchpad.
- 2026-09-21 (a reviewer's positioning note the owner brought in, plus the owner's own ask; branch `home-whole-class`, FOR VIEWING before anything ships): **The home says what Jamyard is before it asks what the class needs.** The reviewer's correction: "student answers become the next step" is the engineering thesis, not the front door, and the four jobs answer "when would I use this", never "what is this". So: (1) the fold's h1 is "Get the whole class in on it." with "Quick activities that get every student participating. No student accounts, nothing to install." under it, and "What does your class need?" moves to an h2 (`.ask`) directly over the planks, which now read as its answer; (2) the ways under each plank label (Support each other, Give feedback, Discuss...) are always visible, no hover (hover is invisible on a Chromebook sweep and to anyone scanning); (3) **the moments**: a row of paper chips under the yard's head, "For when: Five minutes before the bell, The discussion is dying, I want everyone's opinion, They need to know each other, Did they get it?, We finished early, Reset the energy", each a curated handful of ids (`MOMENTS` in the home script), a third filter beside the job chips and the search, tap again to clear; (4) **`when`**, a new top-level activity field (owner: "the hover description is the job it fills rather than what it does"): the classroom moment an activity is for, written for all 15 featured built-ins ("When you need to know what landed before they walk out, from every student, not the four who raise a hand."), carried on the games list (`GAME_CARD_META_FIELDS`), shown by the hover card in place of the description (which stays in the popup), read by the yard search. Guards: `tests/screens/when-lines.test.js` (every featured built-in carries one, house style), the home-15b fold and plank tests updated, a hover-card case. Not done: no editor field for `when` yet (a teacher's copy inherits nothing; the hover falls back to the description), the featured user row Rose, Bud, Thorn lives in the live database so it has no line, and the moment lists are curated by hand in the page.
- 2026-09-21 (the duplicate row, open since August; owner: "if it's a duplicate we don't need it"): **With the database on, games/user/ is never read.** A user game saved to disk before the database (2026-08) was listed beside its migrated row, shadowed the row on load (`loadGame` checked games/user/ before the database), and was re-migrated OVER the row on every restart (`migrateFilesystemGames` was an upsert), so a teacher's edit to that activity was lost at the next boot. Now `resolveGamePath`, `loadGame`, and `listGames` take `{ builtInOnly }` (engine/game-loader.js), the server passes `DB_ENABLED` at the load path and all four list call sites, and the migration is insert-if-absent (`insertUserGameIfAbsent`, db.js), the same shape the recipe migration has had since July. The three routes that edit, feature, or delete a game already checked the database first. Never live (games/user/ is gitignored, so Render has no such file); local only. Tests: three in `tests/engine/game-loader.test.js` (a disk copy lists and loads by default, is invisible with the flag, built-ins still load). Same session: `scripts/eval-designer-prompts.js` run live over the six new bricks (the matcher leg had not been): 23 of 25 on the matcher (the two misses are the baseline pair, folded-story-tail to noMatch and vocab-riddles to Creative Vote), 14 of 14 on the storyboard leg.
- 2026-09-21 (owner, on the live site: "everyone drew a picture of me and then we would vote on which one was the best... instead of having the ability to draw something, you had a text box"): **The matcher stops sending drawing ideas to text recipes.** The Create page runs the recipe matcher before the storyboard, and every recipe's answer box is typed; the owner's idea matched Creative Vote (a text box) once in three local runs and Draw Gallery (a gallery, no vote) twice, so the draw-then-vote storyboard never got its turn. The matcher prompt now carries a DRAWING IDEAS rule: a drawing idea never matches a recipe; a plain gallery ("everyone draws X and we put them on the wall") IS Draw Gallery and a fake-caption game IS Doodle Bluff (the two ready-made sentences appear only when built-ins are offered, the forced refit never sees the rule); a drawing idea with a vote, a favorite, captions, a ranking, or scores is noMatch with a reason that says the step builder makes draw then vote. Live after the rule, three runs each: the owner's idea noMatch 3 of 3 (and the storyboard built announce, draw, vote, end with a drawing pad, twice of twice), the dream-house wall Draw Gallery 3 of 3, the caption game Doodle Bluff 3 of 3 (a first cut sent the plain gallery to noMatch too; the positive Draw Gallery sentence fixed that). Tests: `tests/services/match-recipe-drawing.test.js` (2), golden prompt `draw-me-and-vote` (no acceptAlso: the vote is the heart).

(2) The role picker's hint reads "Tap the role you want, you can switch until the teacher locks it in." and the projector's board button "Confirm roles" (team-split keeps its own two, and the team hint, untranslated until now, has rows too); five languages each. Tests: `tests/engine/partner-note.test.js` (8); `scripts/simulate-debate-pairs.js` now checks the partner piece rides beside the prompt (19 of 19); both screens re-shot in the headless Edge.
- 2026-09-20, late night, third open item: **The new screens in a real browser, and one projector fix.** A headless Edge held the projector (`/host`, the fixture injected into the picker) and one student (`/player`, joined by the form) while sim-harness bots filled the class and the projector's own buttons drove the room (scratchpad script, three scenarios). Seen: the drawing ballot as thumbnails two to a row on the student screen, the gallery's Reveal next, the crown with Ada's drawing full size and the standings under it (the crown's pop-in starts a quarter second after the drumroll ends, so an early capture caught a blank; the phase then auto-advances after ten seconds); the pairs round one with "You are arguing For." and "One classmate will read this.", round two with the partner's opening under the instruction and the side kept; the roles menu and the projector's role board. **Found and fixed:** the projector's copy of a pairs prompt printed `{{pair-write.side}}` and `{{pair-write.partner}}` raw, because `resolveTemplate` (server.js) only knew mine and assigned; the three pair tokens now read as projector notes there ("their side", "the other side", "(each student sees what their partner wrote)"), guarded by `tests/engine/host-prompt-notes.test.js`. Two nits left in NEXT-STEPS: the partner's piece renders in the same heavy prompt style as the instruction (a paper quote would set it apart), and the role-choice screens still say "Tap the team you want" and "Confirm teams" (they reuse the team-split wording).
- 2026-09-20, late night, second open item: **The vote over drawings, and a crown after every storyboard vote.** A ballot showed only text, so "draw a monster and vote for the scariest" had no honest path. Now a drawing step's responses ride to the ballot with their strokes: the vote handler thins each drawing first (`thumbnailStrokes` in `engine/drawing.js`, at most 24 points per stroke and 600 per drawing, so thirty drawings on one ballot stay under half a megabyte; the collect step keeps the full strokes), the student ballot draws a thumbnail canvas per candidate (`fillVoteButton` in player.js, pick-one and head-to-head alike, `.vote-btn-drawing` two to a row), the winner lookup carries a drawing entry with its strokes instead of skipping it (`findWinnerEntries`), the engine adds `winnerDrawing` to the crown, and the projector draws the winning entry full size under the crown (`.winner-drawing`, replayed once) whenever the entries list carries strokes, tie or not. Storyboard: a vote after a draw or collect step sets `excludeAuthors` (nobody votes for their own) and is followed by a winner step reading its scores, because a tallied vote with nothing after it showed the class no winner (Creative Vote always had the crown; the storyboard's vote brick never did); a vote over the AI's literal options gets no crown, and its builder now sets `question` instead of `prompt` (the literal storyboard vote had failed the validator since the brick was written). Prompt: the vote line says the ballot shows drawings as thumbnails and the winner is crowned, the draw line routes "vote for the scariest" to a vote right after the draw step, the honesty rule counts a vote over the class's work as a winner source, the show-of-hands wording is gone. Tests: `tests/engine/drawing-vote.test.js` (9), the draw brick tests (vote after draw, vote after collect, literal vote all hostable), golden `monster-wall` now draw then vote. Proof: `scripts/simulate-drawing-vote.js` (20 checks on `games/_sim-drawing-vote`: every ballot holds the other three drawings thinned, the crown names the winner and the projector gets all 100 points of the long stroke). Live: the monster idea built 3 of 3 as announce, draw, vote, end, clean.
- 2026-09-20, late night (owner: "let's continue the list", the probe's open items): **The answer to a guess, from the console.** A teacher's private number (the jar count, known only after the guesses are in) had no door: an estimate step without an answer was a poll for good. Now, while a Guess the Number step is open, the console shows **The answer** (a number box, Set the answer, Enter works); `estimate-set-answer` (teacher-only, open step only, stale-guarded, a `number:required` schema) writes the open step's answer, every console hears `teacher-estimate-answer`, and the existing close scores the closest guess and reveals the number; the console learns a standing answer (typed, or the config's) through `estimateAnswer` on `teacher-phase` and the join snapshot; nothing reaches students before the close. The guide's live-controls list has the line. The storyboard's estimate brick now keeps `unit` and `scoring` without an answer (the teacher may still type one), and its prompt rule says: with no answer, write that the teacher enters the real count and the closest guess takes it (the earlier "no winner promised" rule is retired, the door makes the promise true). Tests: `tests/engine/estimate-answer-event.test.js` (5, wire + console + server guards), the estimate brick tests updated. Proof: `scripts/simulate-estimate-answer.js` (10 checks over two rooms on `games/_sim-estimate-answer`, compiled by the brick).
- 2026-09-20, night, sixth and last item: **The summarize brick.** "Everyone answers, then the answers get summed up into three themes for the board" was declined 3 of 3 ("nothing sorts them into categories") while ai-process summarize had done it since the start (Discussion Starter, the Builder's AI pair). The brick `{ brick: "summarize", text, heading? }` compiles that pair through `buildAiPair` (task summarize over the last collect, the reveal of `{{ai.result}}` under the heading); the prompt describes it and says the words the class reads never name the AI (the standing rule; the wait screen and footer already say who reads the answers); the concierge list and validator carry `heading`. Open from the probe: a door for a teacher's private number before an estimate reveal; a vote or captions over drawings from a storyboard (Doodle Bluff stays a recipe).
- 2026-09-20, night, fifth item: **The draw brick.** "Draw a monster, put them on the wall, vote for the scariest" was declined 3 of 3 as typed-text-only while Draw Gallery already did the drawing half. The brick `{ brick: "draw", text, gallery?, timer? }` compiles Draw Gallery's shape from the AI's words: a drawing collect (timer default 90), the teacher preview gate (reject = redraw; nothing student-drawn reaches the projector without it), and a reveal-one gallery from the drawings. A vote ballot shows only text, so a vote fed by a drawing step is refused with a plain problem, and the prompt says the favorite is the class's show of hands during the gallery (the first cut still declined 3 of 3, treating the vote as the heart; the rule now says BUILD the draw step and never cantBuild for a missing drawing vote, and the re-run built 3 of 3 with a show of hands in the gallery line every time). The concierge list and validator carry `gallery`. Tests: `tests/screens/draw-brick.test.js` (7, the audience line reads "the class after your teacher reviews it"), golden prompt `monster-wall` (Draw Gallery accepted). Still true: drawings cannot be voted on, captioned by classmates, ranked, or scored from a storyboard; Doodle Bluff's captions stay a recipe.
- 2026-09-20, night, fourth item: **The estimate brick's answer.** "Closest guess wins" built an estimate with no answer and no scoring, and the words promised a winner anyway. The estimate phase already reveals the answer, the class spread, and closeness-ranked scores at close when it has one; the brick now carries `answer` (a number), `unit`, and `scoring` closest | graduated, and the compiler leaves unit and scoring off when there is no answer (poll mode). The prompt's honesty rule lets an answered estimate promise the closest guess; the estimate line tells the AI to write a number only when the teacher named it or it is certain (never its own guess), and, when the number is the teacher's own (their jar), to leave the answer out AND stop promising a winner (the first cut left the answer out honestly 3 of 3 but still wrote "closest guess wins" 3 of 3; the rule now names the phrases and the poll-mode fact, and the re-run was honest 3 of 3, "when the teacher reveals the count"). The concierge list and validator carry the three fields. Tests: `tests/screens/estimate-answer-brick.test.js` (6), golden prompt `closest-guess-heartbeats` (Estimation Station accepted). Live: the heartbeats idea built 3 of 3 with answer 100000, beats, closest. Open: a teacher's private number (the jar count) has no door yet; the make page or the console could take it before the reveal.
- 2026-09-20, night, third item: **The roles brick.** "Groups of four, a job each, then a shared to-do list" had compiled to teams > rank byGroup > assign, a broken lookalike that validated clean (a hand-out gives one item per GROUP, so every group got one Facilitator while the announce promised a job per member). The engine has team-roles and checklist; the brick `{ brick: "roles", roles: 2-8 names, method?: random | choice, tasks?: string[], text? }` compiles a team-roles step over the last teams step (or the last pairs step, a pairwise collect counts as groups) and, with tasks, a checklist with `rolesFrom` whose "Job: task" lines the checklist already tags at game time; text is the list's instruction. The storyboard prompt describes it, tells the hand-out rule it is for ONE option per group and never for jobs, and forbids rank + assign for member jobs; the concierge list and validator carry `roles`, `method`, `tasks`. Tests: `tests/screens/roles-brick.test.js` (6), golden prompt `lab-jobs-checklist` (Group Work Day accepted as the matcher's answer). Live: built 3 of 3 (4.5 to 5.7 s), teams then roles every time, eight tagged tasks.
- 2026-09-20, night, second item: **Video on the bricks.** "Watch this YouTube clip, then answer two questions" was the other probe idea a teacher got nothing for, while announce, collect, and collect-choice already carry a `video` field the projector plays. The three bricks now take `video`; the compiler passes only a YouTube link through (the same five shapes `engine/video.js` embeds, in `YOUTUBE_LINK`) and drops anything else with a plain problem; reveal and end ignore it. The storyboard prompt names the field on the three bricks and routes a pasted link onto the opening announce (a non-YouTube link is an honest cantBuild); the concierge list and validator carry it. Tests: `tests/screens/video-brick.test.js` (5), golden prompt `clip-then-questions`. Live: built 3 of 3 (3.0 to 3.5 s), the link on the announce every time.
- 2026-09-20, night (owner: "Let's do it in order", the probe's ranked list; branch `storyboard-bricks` on top of PR #57): **The pairs brick.** Debate pairs was the one idea a teacher got nothing for (noMatch upstream, an honest decline downstream) while the engine already paired students and kept pairs across steps. Engine: three per-recipient tokens join mine/assigned in `engine/per-player-template.js`, `{{X.partner}}` (what the partner wrote at pairwise step X; a triple hears both, one after the other; a partner who passed or never wrote reads an i18n line in every table), `{{X.side}}` and `{{X.partnerSide}}`; collect gains `sides: [A, B]` (schema, validator + editor mirror: exactly two labels, pairwise only), dealt in `buildPairwiseAssignment` through `dealSides` (pairing.js: one each per pair, a triple two of the first) and stored on the step's data; the grammar knows the suffixes (`PER_PLAYER_SUFFIXES`); the audience line reads a prompt quoting `{{X.partner}}` as one classmate. Brick: `{ brick: "pairs", text, rounds?: string[] (0-3), sides?: [A, B], timer? }` compiles to a pairwise collect (oddHandling triple, nobody sits out a debate), one collect per round with `reusePairsFrom` the first and the partner's latest piece under the instruction (unless the text places `{{partner}}` itself), a bold side line under each prompt when sides are dealt (unless the text places `{{side}}`), and a pair-private reveal of the last exchange; the AI's plain `{{side}}` / `{{otherSide}}` / `{{partner}}` tokens are bound to the step by the compiler. The storyboard prompt describes it, no longer bans partner exchanges, and routes "swap with a partner / rebuttal / argue then switch" to ONE pairs step with the question inside the pairs text (the first live runs collected the question in a step nothing read); the concierge list and validator carry `rounds` and `sides`. Tests: `tests/engine/pair-tokens.test.js` (16), `tests/screens/pairs-brick.test.js` (6), golden prompt `debate-pairs`. Proof: live, the debate idea built 3 of 3 with the brick (4 to 6 s), and `scripts/simulate-debate-pairs.js` (18 checks) played `games/_sim-debate-pairs` (compiled by the brick) with five students: sides dealt 3/2, the pair opposite, each round showing the partner's piece and never the student's own, the triple seeing both partners, the switch naming the other side, the reveal pair-private with nothing on the projector.
- 2026-09-20, evening (owner: "test the storyboard and see if we're able to make things that we have the components to make", then "This is a good list of repairs. Let's get started"): **The storyboard probe, and the first two repairs.** 27 teacher-typed challenges (10 controls, 14 where the engine has the mechanic but the storyboard has no brick for it, 3 honest declines), 3 storyboard runs each on the production setting (Sonnet 5 low) plus one matcher run each, every built plan compiled and validated: 63 of 81 built, all clean; 4 of 81 JSON parse errors; every decline honest; the report with the ranked gap list is `docs/research/storyboard-probe-2026-09-20.md`. Repairs: (1) **`_parseStoryboard` closes what the model left open.** Every captured failure ended in `}]` with the object's own `}` missing (chain plans most of all); the regex fallback trimmed to the end step's brace and died at the array. New `closeUnbalancedJson` (a string-aware bracket stack that appends the missing closers) runs as a rung after the regex, then the quote repair over the balanced text; the captured failing reply now parses. (2) **A recipe id in the matcher's game slot is a recipe pick.** "Closest guess wins" came back as `{"game": "estimation-station"}` three times out of three; the route called it an unknown activity and said noMatch, so the teacher fell to a storyboard that cannot score guesses. The mirror of the 2026-09-07 rule in `matchRecipe`. Tests: `tests/services/storyboard-parse-repair.test.js` (9), `tests/services/match-recipe-recipe-in-game-slot.test.js` (4). Still open from the probe, in the report's order: a pairs brick (debate pairs gets nothing today: noMatch upstream, declined downstream), video on the announce brick (same), a roles brick or a rule against teams > rank > assign for per-member jobs (a broken lookalike that compiles clean), an answer on the estimate brick, a draw brick, a summarize brick.
- 2026-09-20, later (owner: "Let's do a snappyness pass outside of ai"): **The snappiness pass, measured first.** Every teacher page timed on the live site over CDP, cold and warm, unthrottled and on a simulated school wifi (60 ms, 4 Mbps): cold loads ran 0.3 to 0.9 s unthrottled and 1.0 to 2.7 s on school wifi, warm loads 0.13 to 0.29 s everywhere (Render already serves brotli; `max-age=0` means a warm load is 25 to 39 revalidations, cheap). The socket round trips against the live site: connect 150 to 300 ms, and with the default polling-then-upgrade the first emits rode HTTP (create-room 80 to 210 ms, join 80 to 95 ms) where a socket does them in about 30. Four fixes, all free: (1) **the games list is scoped to the visitor** (`GET /api/games?mine=a,b,c`, `engine/games-list-scope.js`, `db.listUserGamesByIds`): it carried every teacher's saved activity to every home, make, Create, and Try it out load, 96 of 143 rows and 60 of 92 KB on the live site, when a visitor can only see built-ins, the owner's featured rows (by override or by a saved `featured: true`, rose-bud-thorn), and their own copies (`MyGames` ids); the reply now also carries `ids`, every id on the server, which is the one thing the pages still needed the whole list for (picking a copy id nobody has taken; `refreshKnownIds` had been reading the list as an array and silently doing nothing); no `mine` at all still means everything (the owner's library console, `/library`); 97 KB became 36 KB on the dev branch, and the Neon query reads a handful of rows instead of all of them. (2) **The class pages load the minified socket client** (`/socket.io/socket.io.min.js`, 15 KB gzipped against 38) and (3) **connect websocket first** (`io({ transports: ['websocket', 'polling'], tryAllTransports: true })` on host, player, teacher, and Try it out; polling stays the fallback for a network that blocks websockets): create-room and join went from 80 to 210 ms to about 30 on the live site. (4) **The two fonts are preloaded** on every teacher and class page (they started only after the stylesheet had parsed, and were the slowest resources after the games list) and **cached for a year** (`/shared/fonts`, versioned file names, `maxAge: '365d', immutable`). Guard: `tests/screens/snappy-pass.test.js` (26) + `tests/engine/games-list-scope.test.js` (7). Known and untouched: `elimination-tournament` appears twice in the list (a filesystem user game and its migrated database row, an open item since 2026-08-27). Not done: bundling the per-page scripts (25 to 39 requests a page; no build step is the rule), a cache on the built-in card list (`listGames` is 30 to 45 ms), the analytics config and track calls on load (async, off the critical path).
- 2026-09-20 (owner: "how much snappier could jamyard be using a different ai model", then "do items 1 through 3 as one PR"): **Snappier AI, measured first.** Every real prompt was timed through AIService against Haiku 4.5, Sonnet 5 at medium / low / no thinking, Opus 5 at low, and fast mode (unavailable: the org's fast-mode limit is zero). No model swap moved the numbers: the Haiku paths (projector summary, recipe match, fit questions) sit at 1 to 2.5 s and every swap made them slower; the writers (quiz, revise) are bounded by writing 300 to 900 tokens back, the same at any effort; over six ideas the storyboard was the one slow beat (a 29 s thinking outlier at medium), and Opus 5 there was not better, it built the pair-students lookalikes the prompt forbids where Sonnet honestly declined. Gemini 3.8 Flash was not benchmarked (no key) and public numbers put its time to first token well above the median. So three levers instead of a model: (1) **the storyboard call runs at effort low** (4 to 9 s on the hard ideas, same declines); (2) **the storyboard streams**: `POST /api/games/storyboard/stream` writes server-sent events (thinking summary slices, the name once whole, each step once its JSON closes, then `done` carrying exactly the plain route's body) from `generateStoryboard(description, {onEvent})` over the new `_streamClaude` twin of the chokepoint, `engine/storyboard-partial.js` finds the whole parts of a half-written reply, `screens/shared/sse-reader.js` reads a POST stream in the browser, and the Create page's plan dialog shows the thinking line, the name, and the steps landing (`.sb-arriving`), then swaps to the editable rows as before; the plain route stays (shared `storyboardOutcome`, one ideas-log write either way); (3) **the revise and recipe-match system prompts are cached** (`cache: true` at `_prepareParams`, one ephemeral block carrying the style rules): about 90% off their input on a hit, no speed change (measured: 6.6 to 8.2 s cached vs 6.2 to 6.9 s uncached), a miss costs 25% more, Haiku needs 4096 tokens to cache so the shorter prompts are left alone. Tests: `tests/engine/storyboard-partial.test.js` (12), `tests/screens/sse-reader.test.js` (8), `tests/services/storyboard-stream.test.js` (10). Proof on :3005 with the real key: the stream route delivered the name at 0.6 s and six steps over the next two seconds; two CDP screenshots of the dialog mid-stream and finished. Not done, on purpose: Opus 5 for the storyboard, a second vendor, folding Ask AI's Haiku triage into the Sonnet revise (it would make every chat turn a 19k-token Sonnet call and lose the distilled edit request that makes Just do it work).
- 2026-09-19, hotfix after the five merges (#51, #52, #48, #49, #50 in that order): **db.js was spliced by the merge and nothing caught it.** #48 and #50 both appended functions after `setFeedbackStatus`; GitHub's three-way merge of #50 treated the shared tails (`RETURNING id`, the closing braces) as context and interleaved `addIdeaLog` into `addRoomLog` and `markIdeaLogSaved` into `updateRoomLog`. The suite stayed green because no test imported db.js, CI deployed, the new server could not start, and Render kept the #49 deploy live (checked: jamyard.org answered 200 and served the terms build throughout). Fix: db.js rebuilt from the post-#48 file plus the idea-log blocks, verified by parse, a real boot on :3005, and both log APIs answering. Guard: `tests/boot-imports.test.js` imports db.js and every module in `services/` and `engine/`, so a syntax error or broken import fails the suite before it can deploy. Lesson for the rebase-and-merge chain: after resolving additive conflicts, boot the server, not only the tests.
- 2026-09-19 (owner: "I want to collect data on: when someone tries to create something; what they made that is unique from what we have; what they tried to make that we weren't able to make"): **The ideas log.** PostHog already counts Create-page outcomes (`create_result`) but its allowlist bans free text on purpose, so the idea itself now lives in our own store: `services/idea-log.js` (Neon `idea_log`, `data/idea-log.ndjson` in local dev), one row per try with the idea as typed (`scrubPatterns` removes emails, phones, links; 2000 chars), the stage (`match`, an `alternate` card re-run, or `storyboard`), the result (`match` / `existing` / `none` / `storyboard` / `cant-build` / `error`), what it pointed at (recipe or built-in id and name), why not (the matcher's or builder's reason, or the validation errors), a storyboard's bricks, the minutes the teacher asked for, and the browser's analytics id (`jamyard.aid`, sent as `aid` by designer.js). Written by `logIdea` at every return of `POST /api/games/from-description` and `/api/games/storyboard` (awaited, so the response carries `ideaId`); the Create page threads `ideaId` into its `POST /api/games` save (the match preview's save and the storyboard's Build it), and the server marks the row `saved_game_id` + `saved_name`, so "what they made" is what they kept. **`/ideas`**, owner-only like `/feedback` and `/rooms` (`ownerAreaGate`, `GET /api/idea-log`): six tiles (tries, matched a recipe, already in the yard, built from steps, could not build, saved), four tabs (All tries / Built from steps = unique / Could not build / Saved), one paper entry per try with the idea, what it became or why not, and the saved name; alternate re-runs indented. Privacy table gets a row (teacher-typed, owner-only, "do not type student names into an idea"). Tests: `tests/services/idea-log.test.js` (8). Proof on :3005 against the dev branch with one real matcher call and one real storyboard call.

- 2026-09-18 (owner: "I need 50 users by october 5th"; the pilot needs per-room truth and a count, and the product said nothing to a teacher who liked it): **The rooms log, the pilot count, and "send a colleague the link".** (1) `services/room-log.js`: one row per real room (never robot playtests), opened on `create-room` and updated on every join, every step, the end, and the host-never-came-back close: activity (a built-in's slug or the teacher's own name for a custom one), room code, kind (class, or Try it out's pretend students, `pretend: true` on the create payload), headcount (only ever grows), step reached out of the top-level steps (a foreach's sub-phases keep the last top-level position), status open / ended / closed, minutes, and a **host key**: a random hex id the host page mints in its own localStorage (`jamyard.hostKey`) and sends inside `create-room`, refused unless it is that shape. Neon table `room_log`; `data/room-log.ndjson` in local dev; a restored room carries `logId` and `kind` in its snapshot and keeps writing to its row. (2) **`/rooms`**, owner-only like the feedback inbox (`ownerAreaGate`, `GET /api/room-log`, `?since=YYYY-MM-DD`): the headline is THE pilot number, distinct host keys with a class room of three or more real students since 2026-09-18 (`summarizeRoomLog`, `PILOT_START`), with rooms opened / class rooms / reached the end / pretend rooms beside it and the last 50 rooms in a table (textContent only; the activity label can be teacher text). (3) Analytics: every room event carries `kind` (class | pretend) and a new **`room_abandoned`** (step of steps, headcount, minutes) fires when a room closes without reaching its end, so a stall shows where it stalled; the privacy page's usage row, a new rooms-log row, and the local-storage paragraph say so. (4) The teacher console's end of activity gets a second card under the report reminder: "Know a teacher who would like this?" with **Copy the link**, which copies the home page with `utm_source=colleague&utm_medium=console` (already an allowlisted tag), so a visit that came from a colleague's copy is visible in PostHog. Proof: `tests/services/room-log.test.js` (10), the allowlist tests, and a live run on :3005 against the dev branch (a sim Snowball room at step 4 of 6 with no key, two Live Poll rooms under one key counted as one host, a pretend room and a two-student room excluded, a malformed key stored as nothing). Not proven live: the closed status and `room_abandoned`, which wait on the five-minute host grace.

I'm not sure what that means or how to set that up."): **AGPL-3.0, set up.** `LICENSE` is the GNU Affero General Public License v3 text from gnu.org, verbatim; `package.json` says `AGPL-3.0-only` (it said ISC, npm's default) and names the repository and homepage; a first `README.md` (what Jamyard is, how to run it, where things are, and a License section in plain words: run, study, change, share for any purpose; share changes under the same license; the network clause means a hosted changed copy must offer its source, which is why the AGPL and not the GPL). The README also says what the AGPL does NOT cover: the prompt banks (the Along bank reproduces "Along Reflection Questions" with per-prompt authors and names no license of its own), the Jamyard name and look, and student work (never in the repo). - 2026-09-18 (owner: "when you click in a box for the typing of a letter, it highlights or something, so the person knows that clicking there worked", the join form on /player): **The room-code blocks show focus.** The real input rides invisibly over the four blocks and its focus style was switched off, so a tap changed nothing on screen (the name box already showed its ring). Now, while the code input has focus, the empty blocks brighten to paper and the next block to fill gets a solid ink edge, a yellow inner line, and a blinking caret (the first empty slot is either slot 0 or the one right after a filled slot, so pure CSS: `.code-entry:focus-within ... :is(.code-slot-0, .code-slot-filled + .code-slot)`; the caret holds still under reduced motion). `scripts/screenshot.js` gained `SHOT_FOCUS=<selector>` (a real mouse press plus focus emulation, since `el.click()` never focuses a box and a headless page has no window focus). Test: join-focus (3).
- 2026-09-18 (owner: "during an activity the entire screen should fit so you don't have to scroll down. most projectors fit 16:9"): **The screen that is up fits the projector and the lid.** Measured first: `scripts/audit-fit.js` drives Try it out in headless Edge with the projector frame at 1920x1080 and the student frame at 1366x768, walks activities forward with pretend students, and records scrollHeight against the viewport for every step (28 activities, 474 measurements): 27 overflowed, worst a long Someone's Got You prompt 678px past the wall (fourteen lines at 58px in the 560px column), Doodle Bluff's title round with a drawing under the prompt (143px), Group Work Day's checklist on the student screen (440px), every drawing step on the student screen (100 to 463px, a phone column on a wide lid). Fix: `screens/shared/fit-screen.js`, loaded by the projector and the student screen: on every change (a new section, a mutation inside it, a resize, a font or image landing) the active section goes back to zoom 1, the page is measured, and the CSS zoom that fits is applied (a few rounds, text re-wraps as it shrinks), all inside one frame; `showSection` on both screens fits synchronously so a new step never paints at the wrong size; the projector stops at half size and the student screen at 60% (the words must stay readable), then the page scrolls as before. Plus two targeted rules: a student on a lid 900px or wider gets a 560px column instead of the phone's 400px, and a projector prompt over 160 characters opens to 740px and drops a size. After: every measured step fits (Doodle Bluff 19 of 19, the re-run of the eleven offending activities clean). Tests: fit-screen (11: the zoom math, the wiring, the wide column).
- 2026-09-18 (owner: "I tried playing doodle bluff. I was using the teacher console and it ended the game early. I think it was a bug that might be in other activities too"): **A stray "next step" on an open answer step closes it instead of skipping it.** Reproduced on the real config with a console socket (`scripts/simulate-console-skip.js`): while a collect was open, the console showed "Close submissions" AND a second forward button labelled with the next step ("Review before the class sees it" on the drawing step); the second one sent `advance-phase`, which moved the room on without gathering, so the drawings were never stored, the rounds had zero items, and the room ran preview, scoreboard, gallery, end in one breath. Every activity with an answer step had the same trap (a skipped collect leaves the next reveal blank). Fix in two places: the close-submissions body is now `closeCollect(code, room)` in server.js (idempotent through `phaseState.closingCollect` while a close waits on in-flight answers), and `advance-phase` on an open collect or collect-choice runs it, so any forward press stores the answers and moves on; the console hides its second forward button while an answer step is open (Close submissions is the one control, its title already says "and move on"). The sim fails 8 of 13 checks on the old server (approve lands on the scoreboard) and passes clean on the new one; teacher-console, submit-race, and the universal Doodle Bluff playthrough still pass.

No student accounts.", which the owner wanted kept after a first cut changed both): "Connect, think, review, or just have fun.", no sub line (an empty `sub` renders nothing). The caption boxes sit above the footage: `.square .caption` at top 84px, the footage composited at y=300 in `FORMATS.square.video` and the ground overlay (the two must agree). Every shot in the square plan got about a beat and a half more (`square.dur` overrides: hook 5.0, make 4.4, code 3.4, connect and think 4.6, review 4.4, fun 5.0, snapped to the 71 BPM grid), 36.4s in all; the wide cut is unchanged at 29.6s. The brief page was the Snowball room's "THREE ROUNDS: ALONE, TOGETHER, ALL OF US." announce flashing for half a second at the end of the lengthened code shot (12s, not 24); the shot starts a second earlier (`square.offset: -3.2`) and ends on the doorway with the names landing. Files overwritten in the owner's Videos folder under the same names. Gotchas: overlay PNGs are cached by their query string (text and kind), not by CSS, so a layout change needs the stale `square-caption-*` and `square-ground-*` images deleted from the take's overlays folder; after lengthening a shot, sheet its frames (`ffmpeg -vf fps=2,scale,tile`) for a page change inside the footage.
- 2026-09-16 (owner: "I want my students who are in teams to pick a first and second choice for one of four categories... each group can decide on their top three choices and then choices are assigned. We weren't able to build it but I think we should be able to do something like this for collective decision-making and choices fairly easily"; branch `choice-draft`): **Collective choices: rank as groups, then hand out the choices.** Why it could not build: the rank step aggregated the whole class only, and nothing in the engine turned rankings into a hand-out, so the storyboard (rightly) said no. Now: (1) `rank` takes an optional `teamsFrom` (a team-split or paired-up collect; every student still ranks on their own screen, each group's order is its members' average by position, stored as `byGroup` + `groupRankedList` beside the class order, the average rule pulled out of `closeRanking` into `engine/phases/choice-draft.js`); (2) a new compute step **`assign`** ("Hand out choices", phase type 31): after a rank step (`from`), every group (or every student when the rank had no groups) gets ONE of the ranked items, first choices first, round by round (round k gives each unit still waiting its k-th pick if that item has a spot; the waiting units go in a fresh shuffled order each round so no group is always served first), spots per item = `perChoice` or the even spread ceil(units / items), anyone whose every pick is full (or who ranked nothing) gets the least-taken item, never left out; output `assignments`, `byPlayer` (so `{{X.mine}}` is your group's item), `byChoice`, `choiceRank`, `assignedList`, and a `board` for the projector; host-paced like the roles deal (one card per group: the item big, "1st choice" / "2nd choice" under it, the members), the student screen says "Your group got: X" (or "You got:"), five language tables carry the rows, the report gets a Group / Choice / Their pick table, the robot playtest continues past `assign-final`; (3) the storyboard has an **`assign` brick** (needs the rank right before it; `perChoice`) and the rank brick a **`byGroup`** flag (a rank followed by assign with a teams step earlier ranks as groups on its own, since that is what the teams were for; no class-order reveal when a hand-out follows), with the storyboard and concierge prompts told to use teams → rank byGroup → assign whenever groups or students should each END UP WITH one option, never a vote or a collect-choice; (4) the **Choice Draft** recipe (#28) and its recipe-born, drift-guarded built-in `games/choice-draft` with the owner's four categories as the default choices (Self and identity, Working with others, Thinking and problem solving, Execution and adaptation), knobs: the choices one box each, the question, who chooses (each group / each student, the split and the group rows behind the switch), students per group, how groups form; the wrap announce reads each student's own item through `{{draft.mine}}`. The editor gets the step (palette under Decide together, a form: Choices ranked in / Spots per item / Projector line, new-step wiring to the last rank, mirror validation) and the rank step a "Rank as groups" select. Team-roles' group resolver moved to `engine/phases/groups-from.js` (shared). Proof: robot playtest of the built-in with 9 bots (teams, rank, assign, wrap, end, no findings) and of the per-student variant with 5, the chaos suite clean; tests: choice-draft (13), assign-validation (6), choice-draft-recipe-born (6), step-suggestions (+4), two golden prompts (the owner's own ask expects the recipe; a chapter draft with a cap expects the storyboard). Not yet seen on a real projector: the assign card layout is the team-split card with three lines. **2026-09-17 follow-up** (owner: "I have mostly groups of three but a couple groups of four; three makes groups of two, four makes too few groups"): the who-chooses knob is three ways, "Groups of a size" / "The groups we already have" / "Each student"; the middle one is a number of groups with `capacity: "open"` on the split (no spot caps, so the teacher arranges uneven groups or students join their own), the size and count rows show behind their own answer, the built-in is restamped (`groups: "size"`). - 2026-09-13 (owner: "I still see zero active users on my dashboard"; branch `analytics-pageview`): **The relay speaks PostHog's page-view dialect, so the built-in dashboards count teachers.** PostHog's Web analytics and "active users" panels count visitors from its own `$pageview` / `$pageleave` events and read `$current_url`, `$pathname`, `$host`, `$referrer`, `$referring_domain`, `utm_*`, and `$session_id`; the relay was sending a custom `page_viewed`, so every panel stayed at zero. `toPostHog` (services/analytics.js) now renames the two page events on the wire and maps their already-allowlisted properties (`path` to `$pathname` + `$current_url` on the served host, `referrer` to `$referrer` + `$referring_domain`, `$direct` for none, the site itself for internal); the allowlist upstream is unchanged and every other event keeps its name. **Visits:** the browser mints a UUIDv7 per tab (`Analytics.sessionId()`, `sessionStorage` `jamyard.sid`, 30 minutes idle starts a new one) and sends it as `session` on every event (a new `uuid` kind), which goes out as `$session_id`; replay.js seeds the recorder with the same id (`bootstrap.sessionID`) so a recording lines up with its page views. **`page_left`** (`$pageleave`, the route only) fires once on `pagehide` or when the tab goes hidden, so a visit gets a duration. `POST /api/track` passes `req.hostname` as the host. Tests: analytics service (+6: the uuid kind, the four mapping cases, the batch shape; the old batch test updated), analytics-surface (+2: the visit id's shape, storage, idle rotation and presence on every event; page_left once with the route only), replay-surface (+1: the seeded session id, stored or live, never a non-UUIDv7).

Masked: every input, every contenteditable box (the make page's question and fields, the Simple view's text), `#chat-messages` (Ask AI), `#my-yard` (the teacher's shelf), `.ph-mask`; blocked: every iframe and `.ph-no-capture`; cross-origin frames, request headers and bodies off. The module runs only on `/`, `/library`, `/make`, `/designer`, `/designer/edit`, `/guide`, never on `/player`, `/host`, `/teacher`, `/prototype`, `/feedback`, `/share`, never inside a frame, never under Do Not Track or Global Privacy Control. Why those six are defensible: the teacher-save purity rule keeps student content off them. Privacy page gained the recordings row; COMPLIANCE-TODO § 1b carries the project-side settings (Record user sessions ON, masking, console and network capture OFF, shortest replay retention). Tests: `tests/screens/replay-surface.test.js` (7: which pages load it after the relay, which never do, the module's fence, the SDK options, the property trim, the masked selectors exist), analytics service (+2: replayConfig), analytics-surface updated (replay.js is the one exempt file in the no-PostHog scan).

- 2026-09-13 (owner: "I want to set up posthog. I need to be careful because I will have minors using the site"; branch `posthog-analytics`): **Site analytics through our own relay; PostHog is a sink, never a script.** No browser loads a PostHog SDK. Teacher pages (home, library, make, create, editor, Try it out, guide, privacy) load `screens/shared/analytics.js`, which posts `{event, props, aid}` to `POST /api/track` (always 204, rate-limited per IP) and refuses to run on `/player`, `/host`, or `/teacher` even if linked there; the student screen, the projector, and the console load nothing. `services/analytics.js` holds the whole schema as an allowlist (`ANALYTICS_EVENTS`): `page_viewed` (a teacher route + the entry point), `activity_opened` (which door on the make page, whether the class fit was used), `create_result` (match / existing / none / storyboard / error), and the server's own `room_created`, `activity_started`, `activity_ended` (a built-in's slug or "custom", headcount, minutes, under a random per-room id, never the code) and `feedback_sent` (the category). Batches go to PostHog's `/batch/` with `$process_person_profile: false` and `$geoip_disable: true`, so no person profiles and no geo; the only IP PostHog can see is Render's. The browser id is random hex in localStorage; Do Not Track and Global Privacy Control turn the module off. Off without `POSTHOG_KEY` (`POSTHOG_HOST` picks the US or EU cloud). Robot playtests and restored rooms send nothing. The privacy page's retention table gained the usage-events row and the local-storage paragraph names the id; `docs/COMPLIANCE-TODO.md` § 1b lists the owner's PostHog project settings (discard IP, replay off, retention, DPA, subprocessor notice). - 2026-09-13 (owner: "the customization, with More, is now hidden too much"; four directions on a design canvas, the owner picked a card-plus-panel, saw it built, and said "it's asking the same question twice on the same page... more reading than I would like"; the second try, rows of chips, "is closer, let's try it"; branch `make-setup-card`): **Make it fit your class: rows of chips on the make page, nothing folded, and the AI asks one question, two at most, as taps where it can.** The More fold is gone. `.fit-section` (screens/make/) is a headed paper card under the doors, above What happens; every setting is ONE row (`.fit-row`, a grid: the question in a 190px column, the answer in `.fit-a` where chips wrap among themselves): the AI's one or two questions first, then Your class (a chip with the saved profile, short: "9-12 · English / ELA +1", or "Not set", and a "change" / "set it" link that shows the class picker under the rows; the picker's own Done hides it), Student names (Shown / Hidden), Early-bird joke (On / Off). The question is the only text on a row: no tally, no instructions, no Done. **The questions have a kind** (`AIService.shapeCustomizeQuestion`, pure): `choice` when the answer is a SHAPE the model can name in advance (answer length, tone, which way round, keep it open or focus), a row of `.fit-chip`s with "Type your own…" (dashed) opening the plank; `text` when the answer is a specific THING only the teacher knows (the words, the book, the unit, the facts), the plank (`.fit-plank`) in the row with the mic on it. Each carries a `label` (unused by the rows now, kept for the card the dialog may want); a choice question with fewer than two choices becomes text; a choice question that LISTS its choices after a colon loses the clause (a real run asked "...: short phrases, full sentences, or a mix?" over the same three chips); placeholders cap at 70 (a long one clipped on the one-line plank). The prompt asks ONE question the wording hangs on and a SECOND only if the content truly depends on it, never a third (parser caps at 2), one question for the WHOLE activity, never one per round (Vocab Match asked "round 1 pairs" and "round 2 pairs"), under ten words, about the WORDS only: never timing, timers, minutes, rounds, group sizes, grade, or subject (a real run asked "how long should students spend writing alone" with Timer in knownSettings). The questions load with the page and again when the class changes; a request counter drops a stale reply. A quiz or bluff panel still asks nothing (`state.noQuestions`). The mic wraps the plank (speech-input.js), so hiding a plank hides its wrapper too. The old dialog (make-it-yours.js) reads the same fields it always did; the new ones are additive. Verified over headless Edge with the real model: Snowball = one choice question (tone) in one row; Vocab Match = the language pair and the focus, both typed. The request now names the print's words as fixed ("The teacher wrote the question in step X themselves: ... Keep it word for word", the field labels too) and fits everything else to that question AND the answers; verified against the real model (the question kept, the intro reworded casual). **And What happens follows the edits** (owner: "is there a way that What happens can populate a preview based on your responses?"): two tiers. The map redraws from the edited copy as the question, the field labels, or the timer change (`scheduleMap`, 400ms; `POST /api/games/:id/make` now returns `map` beside the config, no AI), so the Open answer stop quotes the new question at once. And one button under the rows, **See how it reads** (shown once an answer is in), runs the AI fit ONCE, redraws the map from the fitted copy via the new `POST /api/games/map` (the pure builder over a posted config, nothing stored), and keeps the copy (`state.fitted`, keyed on the edits + answers) so TRY IT / Host it now open it without a second AI call; any later change puts the button back ("See how it reads now"). Never runs on its own: one Sonnet call per press, never per tap. Verified headless with the real model: a typed question landed in the map in under a second; "Serious and formal" turned the announcement into "This exercise proceeds in three stages: individual reflection…" with the question untouched. `scripts/screenshot.js` gained `SHOT_EVAL` (run a snippet before the capture). **Owner's Live Poll test, same day:** the AI asked for the answer choices, the owner typed a new question into that row, pressed See how it reads, and "the question at the top didn't change": the fit had rewritten the choices and the map, but the print never redrew, and the reword had pinned the question as the teacher's even though they had not touched it. Replayed headless with the real model: the answer became the question at the top, the choices became Got it / Mostly got it / A little confused / Lost, the map matched. **Same day, Speed Quiz:** "it's really important to be able to customize questions", so a recipe's own panel (the quiz's questions, the bluff's facts, Doodle Bluff's knobs) sits right under the doors now, above the rows and above What happens (the page reads print, doors, the questions, fit rows, map). **And the yard's door says PICK THIS ONE too** (owner: the home said Pick this one, the yard said Make it yours, "I think we're going with pick this one"): both library.js sites (the card and the popup) read "Pick this one"; "Make it yours" stays the name of the flow, the dialog title, and the make page. **Vocab Match, same day** (owner: "there should be a way to preview the pairs, similar to the quiz ones"): **a pairs panel** under the doors on any activity with match steps whose pairs are plain text (`pairsFor` in engine/make-print.js rides on the print payload as `pairs`, one round per match step, labelled by the step's name or "Round N"; tuples read too; a round with a {{token}} in a pair is left to the designer). - 2026-09-12 (owner built "Guess the Class" on the live site: "on a scale of 1 to 10" gave a number box with no bounds, and the design chat claimed a slider): **An estimate step with a known range is a row of numbers to tap, or a slider; the range comes from the step or from the question's own wording.** `engine/phases/estimate-range.js`: `inferRange(prompt)` reads "on a scale of 1 to 10", "from 1 to 5", "between 0 and 100", "a 1-10 scale", "out of 10"; `effectiveRange(phase)` lets explicit `min`/`max` win and fills what they leave open from the wording; `clampGuess` is the server clamp. The estimate handler sends that range on start and reconnect, and `estimate-submit` clamps into it, so the owner's existing activity (no min/max on the step) gets the scale without an edit. Student screen (`renderEstimatePicker`): up to twelve whole numbers are a row of painted buttons (`.scale-pick`, the pick goes yellow, aria-pressed), a longer range up to 1000 is a slider with the number box as its readout (typing moves the slider too), an open guess stays a typed box; the picker locks with the box on close and on the timer, and the bench's bot fill taps a number on the scale instead of typing 1..200. Storyboard: the estimate brick carries `min`/`max` through `validateSuggestions` (finite numbers only) and `compileStoryboard` (max > min), and both AI brick docs (the concierge's allowed-bricks line and the storyboard's brick list) say a scale question MUST set them; the estimate schema description says what a range does. Golden prompt `guess-the-class-scale` compiles the reference. Verified headless on a fresh server with the owner's live config: ten buttons 1 to 10, the typed box hidden, a tap lands in the input. Tests: `tests/engine/estimate-range.test.js`, `tests/screens/estimate-scale.test.js`, two validator cases; 1917 total.
- 2026-09-12 (owner's ask: "when you click Host, the teacher console opens in a new tab"): **Host opens the projector in a new tab, in front, and the private teacher console in the tab Host was pressed in, already signed in.** `screens/shared/host-launch.js`: a new tab always takes focus, so the projector goes there and the teacher keeps looking at the host view (the first cut sent the console to the new tab and the owner landed on the console: "I don't want to have to tab back"). The new tab has to open inside the click (browsers block `window.open` after an await), but the room, its code and its PIN exist only once the projector page creates it, and some doors save first. So `HostLaunch.begin()` opens a blank tab in the click, `HostLaunch.hostUrl(gameId)` sends that tab to `/host?game=..&pair=<nonce>` and returns `/teacher#await=<nonce>` for this one, the projector publishes `{code, pin}` under the nonce on `room-created` (`HostLaunch.publish`: localStorage, so a console tab that loads AFTER the room is up still finds it, plus a BroadcastChannel for the live case; stale records purged after an hour), and the console tab, listening (`HostLaunch.listen`), fills the form and joins by itself (its button reads "Waiting for the projector…" meanwhile; a minute with nothing heard hands the form back). Same origin, the teacher's own machine, nothing through the server. A blocked popup is not an error: the projector opens as before and Copy teacher link still works. Wired into every Host door: the yard's cards, the Create page's three, the editor's Host (begin before the save, `abandon()` closes the waiting tab if the save fails), the Builder's Host it live now, Try it out's Host this, the make page's Host it now (`clearOpening` abandons on failure or Cancel), and the shared doors module (`door.dest === 'host'`), through `copyDestinationUrl`/`destUrl` for the address. The projector reads the nonce at load (`PAIR_NONCE`), because its address is tidied to `/host` inside the same handler that would otherwise read it; `LAUNCH_URL` drops the nonce so Play again never pairs a dead tab. The lobby checklist's step 3 says "Your teacher console is open in another tab" when it is; the console's join hint and the guide say Host does this. Verified headless over CDP (a user-gesture click on Host it now: a second tab on /teacher, then "Snowball · Room SIVA" joined). Guarded by `tests/screens/host-launch.test.js` (the mechanism with fakes, every door, every page loading the module first); 1902 tests.

Saved to My yard"); its existing-activity match and the search results' Make it yours detoured through the yard's `?customize=` deep link, whose own/owner branch sends an owner-mode browser (the owner's) straight to the editor. On the make page, `isOwn` (`MyGames.has(gameId)`) routes every save through `saveAndGo`: a copy that is already the teacher's is saved with `PUT /api/games/:id` and keeps its name, then opens the door; a built-in still becomes a new copy through `MakeItYours.saveCopyAndReturn`. "Use it as written" goes the same way. - 2026-09-12 (owner tried Play Again in Try it out and nothing happened): **Play Again inside the bench resets and relaunches the bench.** The projector's Play Again is a reload back to the launch address, a new room. Inside Try it out the host frame did exactly that, but the bench had stopped listening after the first `room-created`, so the pretend students, the console tab and the plan rail stayed on the old room and the new one sat empty. Now the host, when it runs inside the bench (`?prototype=true` in an iframe), posts `prototype-play-again` to the parent instead of reloading, and the bench does what its own reset-then-relaunch path does for a changed player count: same activity, same number of students, a fresh room. Verified headless over CDP (`goto=end`, click Play Again in the frame, every student frame joins the new code). Guarded in `tests/screens/bench-logic.test.js`; 1887 tests.

(2) The home's projector mock under 600px stacks the frame body (pile under the prompt, scaled 0.8), prompt at 22px, smaller code blocks and QR; before, `clamp()` floors of 36px in a narrow column wrapped the prompt three letters per line (seen in a 390px render, fixed in the same). `body { overflow-x: clip }` under 960px ends the sideways scrollbar the tilted planks caused (clip, not hidden: hidden would break the sticky chip row). (3) `section.active > button:not([hidden])` and `.preview-actions` on the host are `position: sticky; bottom: 14px`, so a full roster or a long reveal no longer pushes Start or Reveal Next below the fold, on a real projector or inside Try it out's frame; the student's `#submit-btn` is sticky the same way (the draw notice used to push it off a short Chromebook). (4) The doors module says under the front row what Launch opens ("Launch opens two ways to run it: with pretend students, or with your class."), hidden once Launch is open; an outside reviewer never found Try it out behind it. (5) design.css lost the paste-up system: Archivo Black, Nunito and EB Garamond @font-face rules and their six woff2 files, the whole old palette (`--blue`, `--red`, `--pink`, `--shadow-pop`, ...); what stays is `--text`/`--text-soft` (the editor reads them), the blue focus ring as a literal, and the mic button. M still in" instead of a fixed "of N". `runBottomPercent` no longer eliminates everyone when the whole room ties (nobody goes, the round repeats), and both screens say "Everyone tied, nobody is out this round." (i18n rows in all five tables). The universal sim's bots vote at random now (a positional pick made them vote in a ring and tie every round). - 2026-09-10 (classroom bug, found with the teacher console open on the projector machine): **The projector rebinds to its room on every socket reconnect, and the console says when the projector is gone.** The host screen's 'connect' handler bailed out whenever the page URL carried `?game=` (how the yard, Make it yours, and Try it out all launch it), a rule meant for page loads ("a ?game= launch wants a fresh room") that also fired on socket.io's automatic RECONNECT. So a wifi blip, or the browser putting the projector tab to sleep behind the teacher console, dropped the host's socket, the server released `roomToHost`, the client came back with a new socket id and never sent `host-rejoin`: every student who joined afterwards showed on the console (which does rejoin on reconnect) and never on the projector, and five minutes later the room closed. Fix: (1) the decision moved to `screens/host/host-session.js` (pure, tested): a tab that is ALREADY hosting a room (`currentHostToken` in memory) rejoins on every connect whatever the URL says; `?new=1` / `?game=` / prototype rules apply only to a page load. (2) Once a `?game=` launch has its room, the address is tidied to `/host` (prototype iframes excepted) so an F5 or a discarded-and-reloaded tab rebinds instead of minting a new room; Play again goes back to the launch address. (3) The server stamps `hostConnected` on every `teacher-roster` and the join snapshot, and emits a roster on host drop and host rejoin; the console shows a red "The projector screen is not connected. Reload the host tab..." line until it comes back. Proven live on a throwaway :3005 server with a real headless-Edge host page whose transport was forced closed: old client 1 row on the projector after the blip, new client 2. Test count: **1803** (+9, `tests/screens/host-session.test.js`).

Doors: edits go through `POST /api/games/:id/make` (bounded teacher text, applied by the engine module, the copy named "Snowball: What makes an experiment fair?"), an answered More question through the existing reword, and every door saves through `MakeItYours.saveCopyAndReturn`; **untouched = the original runs and no copy is saved**, the designer gets an unsaved draft, as the dialog did. The seconds after a door: the print dims under a yellow "Opening with pretend students…" card (with the honest "about twenty seconds" when the AI is rewording). `MakeItYours.open` now sends every template to the page. **Same day, the owner's ask: the four with their own editors go there too.** The quiz and bluff panels and the generic dialog's setup knobs grew a mount mode (`MakeItYours.mountPanel(panel, game, config, summary, element)`): the same editor the dialog had, rendered as a paper card under the doors ("The questions" for Speed Quiz and Solo Quiz, "The facts" for Trivia Bluff, "Set it up" for Doodle Bluff's phrase source), no title, no doors, the page's TRY IT calls its `makeCopy(dest, {anonymous})` (returns false on a validation problem shown in the panel, a promise while building). The dialogs stay reachable behind `window.MAKE_IT_YOURS_DIALOG`. The audit that shaped this: of 16 shelf templates, 7 are one-question (instant), 7 turn on a knob or a list, 2 are threaded (One More Thing, Both Sides of the Rope: the AI reword stays their path, through More). It compiles to one collect per pile, each later pile rotating from the one before it with `rotateShuffle` and no token in its prompt (a blind hand-off, the hand is shown only at the writing step), then the writing collect over `YOUR PERSON: {{person.assigned}}` lines (leading article dropped), `simultaneousReveal`, and a reveal-one share-out; pile ids come from their labels (`person`, `circumstance`, `pile-3` when the label is junk). The storyboard prompt offers it, routes pool-and-deal ideas to ONE deal step, and says a student's "list of four" becomes one item per pile at class size; the honesty clause names a deal's hand as a place an answer can stay private; the concierge's brick list has it; `suggest-validate` carries `piles` and `writeTimer`. Golden prompt `prompt-generator-deal` (Story Ingredients accepted as the host answer). Fixture `games/_sim-deal` is the golden storyboard compiled; the robot playtest (`simulateGame`) runs it clean with the dealt hand rendered per player. The universal sim skips the first collect after an announce (known false alarm; Story Ingredients shows the same), use the robot for deal shapes. Live: the prompt returns announce, deal (A person / A circumstance), end, and it validates.
- 2026-09-07 (the shuffle-and-deal prompt): **The right activity in the wrong slot.** "Everyone lists people and circumstances, they're shuffled, everyone gets one of each" is Story Ingredients, and the matcher said so, but as `{"recipe": "story-ingredients"}`; the route called that an unknown recipe, fell to the storyboard, and the teacher got "This one needs a trick we don't have yet" (honest for the bricks, which have no deal). `matchRecipe` now reads an id that is not an offered recipe but IS a listed ready-made activity as a game pick, whichever slot it sits in; a real recipe id with a same-named built-in twin (Doodle Bluff) stays a recipe. Live: the prompt lands on Story Ingredients with Idea Chain as the alternate. Not done: a `deal` brick for the storyboard door (Story Ingredients' shape as a brick: 2 to 4 piles, shuffle-dealt, a writing step over the hand), so an idea like this with different piles could be built rather than adapted.
- 2026-09-07 (the fear prompt): **The storyboard door was dead, and two bricks it was missing.** "Everyone shares what they're most afraid of, guess who shared what, then rank them by how scary they are" got "no match" from the recipe matcher (fair: Who Said It? is half of it) and then "Could not sketch the plan: Cannot read properties of undefined (reading 'match')". (1) `generateStoryboard` was the one caller still reading `message.content[0].text`; since the judgment tasks moved to Sonnet 5 on 2026-09-03 the first block is a thinking block, so every real storyboard call had thrown for four days and the tests never saw it (their mock replies put a text block first). It reads through `extractText` now, with a regression test whose mock reply leads with a thinking block. (2) `guess: "who"` on the guessing-rounds brick compiles the Who Said It? shape (`candidateSource: players`, three decoys, a collect-choice over `_candidates`, the author revealed with the class's bar chart; no points, so nothing promises a score); the default stays the typed guess at a secret. The Builder palette has a "Who said it rounds" tile beside Guessing rounds (`who-rounds` in `phase-names.js`). The storyboard rule now splits guess-the-hidden-thing (collect-two) from guess-who (plain collect + guess who).
