- 2026-09-06 (outside review, accessibility): **An axe audit script, and the first pass on what it found.** `scripts/a11y-audit.js` drives headless Edge over CDP, injects axe-core (new dev dependency) into `/`, `/library`, `/designer`, `/prototype`, `/guide`, `/privacy`, `/host?game=snowball`, `/player`, and prints violations by page (impact, rule, help, up to three selectors; `--detail` adds axe's measurements, `--json` saves them; exits 1 on serious/critical). Fixed: the simulator's iframes now carry titles ("Teacher screen", "Player N screen"); the seat picker is a real radio group (container `role="radiogroup"`, one `aria-checked` seat, roving tabindex with arrow/Home/End keys) instead of eight `aria-pressed` buttons that all read "pressed"; `--t-muted-caps` darkened from #8A7B62 to #766A52 (4.5:1 on gesso, 38 of the 78 serious nodes were that one token), the hardcoded copies in designer/prototype/library CSS now read the token; host lobby hints drop the legacy opacity that blended them under 4.5:1; `lang="en"` on the player and designer shells, and `UiLang.set` now writes the activity's language onto `<html>`; `role="main"` on the guide, privacy, home, and simulator wrappers. Left for the design pass (not one-line fixes): white on the brand red/green/cyan/orange buttons and red link text on gesso (3.2 to 3.8:1), the 6px watermark captions on the home mini prints, `lang` on `screens/host/index.html`.
- 2026-09-06: **Doodle Bluff, two ways, and the people who know the answer sit out.** The owner's design: (1) students write the phrases, a classmate draws each, and in that drawing's round BOTH the drawer and the phrase's writer sit out the fake-title step and the vote (they know the truth); (2) the teacher brings the phrase list, each student is handed one, and only the drawer sits out (works for small classes). Engine: `engine/phases/sit-out.js` turns the old scalar `_foreachAuthorId` into a stamped set (`_foreachSitOutIds`, plus `_foreachSourceId` for the words on the holding screen: "This one is yours!" vs "You wrote this one!"), read by every count in lockstep (collect + collect-choice enter/emit/reconnect, submit-response "N of M", close-submissions gather, the teacher console's list which had ignored the author before, and collect-choice's reconnect which used to hand a sitter-out a ballot). The link needed for (1) now rides the response: close-submissions stamps `assignedFromId`/`assignedFromName` next to `assigned` on rotated collects, so `{{_current.assignedFromName}}` names the writer in the reveal. For (2), collect gains `dealItems` (a teacher list handed out one per player in a random order, wrapping; `{{thisStep.assigned}}` shows it; top-level only, never with rotateFrom; validated). Recipe `doodle-bluff` v2: `phraseSource` students|teacher is a `setup: true` knob (Make it yours), `phrases` array param with 24 default phrases, `$if` on the phrases step, the draw step's deal, the intro, and the reveal; `games/doodle-bluff/config.json` is now recipe-born and drift-guarded (`tests/engine/doodle-bluff-recipe-born.test.js`), with `rotateShuffle` on. The "only one option" report: in the simulator the bots answered the phrase prompt and the fake-title prompt from the same bank, so a bot's fake could equal the truth and dedupe it away; bot-brain and the universal sim now draw phrases and fakes from separate banks (drift test), ballots read 3 of 4 with four bots. Owner's simulator run the same evening found the REAL blank-round bug: a student who never submitted a phrase left the classmate downstream of them with no item (the deal skipped that receiver), so that student drew the "(each student gets a different player's item)" placeholder and their round had no truth: raw `{{_current.assigned}}` on the reveal and the placeholder as a ballot option. The deal is now made among submitters only, and every other eligible receiver gets a random submitter's item; a late joiner (or a reconnect under a new id) is dealt one on arrival (`ensureLateAssignment`, rotation and dealItems both). `tests/engine/phases/collect-deal.test.js`. Then the owner STILL saw one option, and the log showed the round handling `guess` before `titles`: **the copy had come back from Postgres with its sub-phases sorted**. `user_games.config` (and `user_recipes.recipe`) were JSONB, which re-sorts object keys by length then bytes, and a foreach's sub-phases run in key order: guess (5) < titles (6) < reveal-truth (12), so every saved Doodle Bluff copy voted before anyone wrote a fake, and the ballot was the truth alone. Built-ins are files, which is why no sim ever hit it. Fix: both columns are now JSON (text as written; idempotent `ALTER ... TYPE JSON USING ::text::json` on init), rows saved under jsonb are repaired on every read from their recipe stamp (`engine/subphase-order.js` `restoreSubPhaseOrder` against a fresh compile; `repairSavedConfig` in server.js wraps every user-game read), the AI revise/chat pipeline carries the original order too (`carrySubPhaseOrder`), and the validator WARNS when a sub-phase reads from a sibling listed after it. **Same phrase, same seat on the bench + a closing gallery** (owner's two follow-ups): a teacher list shorter than the class repeats, and a slow classmate's phrase gets backfilled with a duplicate, so anyone HANDED the same phrase as the drawing on screen knows the answer: `foreachSitOut` now takes every item the round walks (sampled or not) and adds them to the sit-out set (`sameItemIds`, holding line "You were handed the same phrase, so you know this one!"). And the drawings the `limit` sample leaves out are no longer lost: foreach keeps them as `skipped` (foreachState + stored output, schema `skipped: responseArray`), the recipe adds a `gallery` reveal-one over `rounds.skipped` after the scoreboard ("Name: phrase" captions via itemTemplate, which reveal-one now honors for drawings), and a reveal-one with nothing to show advances itself instead of projecting an empty stage. Live-checked: 4 students on a 2-phrase list, drawer + same-phrase classmate sit out, 3 drawings in the gallery. **Three phrase sources** (owner's ask): `phraseSource` students | teacher | ai, recipe v3. Teacher mode now has somewhere to type: setup knobs grew two modes, `{mode: "lines", showWhen: "phraseSource=teacher"}` (an array of strings edited one per line) and `{mode: "text", showWhen: "phraseSource=ai"}` (a short free-text param), with `showWhen` hiding a knob until another knob has the named value (`SetupKnobs.knobVisible`, `.knob-row` wrappers in the dialog, validated in recipe-schema). AI mode compiles an `ai-process` generate `perPlayer` step on the teacher's topic, rotated into the draw step like the students' own phrases; close-submissions stamps `assignedFromId` only for a student-made source, so nobody "wrote" an AI phrase and only the drawer sits out. The mock AI answers a perPlayer generate with a JSON array of N so robot playtests run that mode. Also: the teacher console now shows this round's drawing above the live entries (`displayDrawing` on `teacher-phase` and the snapshot), and the Make it yours door you click reads "Making your copy…" while the AI rewords (the wait read as a dead click).
- 2026-09-06: **Word help**, rationed translation for language classes (a Spanish teacher's request): students tap a word in a prompt they do not know and see it in English, but only N times each. Two systems, one meeting point. **The ledger** (`engine/word-help.js`, 22 tests): top-level `wordHelp: { tokens: N, to: "en" }`, a per-student purse of spends keyed by player id (the reconnect id-migration walker carries it; `room.wordHelp` rides the restart snapshot so a restart never refills purses), server-authoritative, the client only shows the count it is told; a failed lookup refunds; generic on purpose so later rationed help (hints, examples) can draw on the same purse. **The lookup**: `normalizeWord` decides what one word is (letters in any script, inner apostrophes and hyphens, 40 chars), `AIService.translateWord` (Haiku, word + its sentence, source = the activity's resolved language, mock mode echoes) with a per-room cache so the second student who taps "escuela" costs no call, and a tapped-word log that is the teacher's real signal: counts, never who. Wire: `word-lookup` → `word-lookup-result` (that student only, carries `phaseInstanceId`, no stale-phase guard because a lookup is not tied to a step), `teacher-word-help` to the consoles. **Screens**: `screens/shared/word-help.js` wraps every `setRichText` sink's words in tappable spans, a fixed yellow chip shows "N translations left", a paper slip under the tapped word shows the meaning (six new i18n rows in every language table); teacher console gets a "Words the class looked up" block; the report gets a matching table section; the editor Settings gets "Word help" (Off / 1 / 3 / 5 / 10 per student) + "Translate into", mirrored client validation, and the AI chat/revise carry (`carryWordHelp`) keeps the setting through unrelated edits. Nothing reaches the projector. Live-checked against a real room with the real model: escuela → school, second student cache hit, refusals for no tokens and non-words.

Internals keep `simulate`/`sim-` ids, routes and script names; CLAUDE.md's vocabulary rule is revised. The home hero's subline now says what the arrangement is for before how it works: "Everyone answers, classmates build on each other's ideas, and the class result lands on the projector. Pick a template or describe an activity and the AI builds it. Students join with a code, nothing to install." Brand paints stay as they are (owner's call after a current-vs-darker comparison); instead the yard's small text (minutes and hook lines) on the cyan, green, and orange planks is ink at full opacity (5.4, 4.5 and 6.1 to 1) and magenta's stays white at full opacity, so the yard's contrast findings retire without touching a paint. Plank names stay white (the large-text bar). The remaining audit findings are white text on the red action buttons and red links, accepted.

The My yard shelf says where copies live: "Your copies live in this browser. Open one and use Share for a link that works on any device." (5) The editor's Activity Settings and Design with AI panels fold to a slim vertical rail (`screens/designer/panel-toggles.js`, body classes `settings-collapsed` / `chat-collapsed`, remembered per browser), and the step column may grow to 980px; nothing in editor.js or chat-panel.js changed. The host lobby, once the room is open, lists the four things to do (project this screen, students join with the code, open your console via Copy teacher link, Copy report link at the end): the console entry point and the report reminder already existed, the checklist names them at the moment they matter.
- 2026-09-06 (outside review, items 1 and 2): **Submitted means stored, and minutes come from the timers.** (1) The reviewer saw Bot Fill leave the host at "2 of 4 submitted" while every student panel said "Answer submitted", then one contribution per pair. Reproduced headlessly with a slow moderation stub: the student screen flipped to "submitted" before the server answered, so two answers still inside the moderation ladder looked done. The Close itself already waits for held answers (2026-09-04), so the data loss the reviewer saw predates that fix; the optimistic flip did not. Now the server sends `response-accepted` to the submitter after every store (`EVENTS.RESPONSE_ACCEPTED`), and the student screen shows "Sending..." on the button until it lands (text, fields, choice, pass, drawing paths; 12s without word brings the button back with "Not sent yet. Please try again.", rows in all six language tables); a rejection clears the pending state as before. Bot Fill keeps firing through a merge step the way it did through relay, so one click carries both approvals. `scripts/simulate-submit-race.js` is the regression: it spawns its own server and a moderation stub that stalls every second answer, submits four at once, closes immediately, and asserts four acks, 4 of 4 on the host, and two seeds per pair. (2) `engine/duration-estimate.js` (+17 tests): `parseRequestedMinutes` reads the budget out of the idea ("five-minute", "5-10 min" is 10, hours), `estimateDuration` walks the primary path adding timers plus named allowances (join 60s, transition 10s, reading 30s, overrun 15s, untimed input 90s, AI wait 25s, seat move 30s; foreach rounds by limit or class size), `fitToBudget` scales timers to a budget with per-type floors (merge 90s, collect 30s...) on a copy, and `timingReport` writes the teacher's sentence. `/api/games/from-description` returns `timing` on recipe matches (with the trimmed config attached when it runs over) and on ready-made picks (report only); the Create page shows the note under the explanation with a "Trim the timers (about N min)" button that swaps the trimmed copy in. The matcher is told never to claim a timing fit, and asks for a contextual `title` ("Snowball: Causes of WWI", em dashes folded, 60 chars max) that becomes the config name. The reviewer's exact request now answers: Snowball: Causes of WWI, "About 6 minutes even with the shortest timers (Solo 90s to 30s, Pairs 240s to 90s). Your request said 5." 1684 tests.

- 2026-09-04 (owner's list of smaller fixes): **Doodle Bluff's one-title ballot** was a race, not a config bug: submit-response awaits the moderation ladder, and a Close landing in that gap gathered only the answers already stored, so the rotation deal from `phrases` skipped the drawers whose phrase was still in flight and their round had no `{{_current.assigned}}` (the placeholder note showed up as the "only choice"). New `engine/pending-submits.js`: the submit handler takes a hold across its await, close-submissions settles every hold (10s cap) and re-checks the phase before gathering. Found by running the universal sim against a fresh server three times; two of three runs reproduced it. **Trivia Bluff**: (1) every student now sees the same options in the same order, `buildSharedBallot` in collect-choice.js samples (`poolLimit`) and shuffles ONCE per step, keeps the ballot on `room.phaseState.ballot` for reconnects, and removes only the student's own fake from their copy (the host gets the same list; before, the projector list was unshuffled with the truth last); (2) the recipe's live-round instruction and the prepared-facts prompt (`generateBluffFacts`) both ban numeric blanks and self-solving sentences (the Jim Henson "___ years from 1955 until 1990" case), `games/trivia-bluff/config.json` recompiled from the stamp; (3) the design chat prompt now says outright that writing content (questions, facts, prompts, choices) IS an edit and never to answer "I can only brainstorm"; `summarizeConfigForChat` lists the recipe's settings so it knows facts come live vs prepared. **Next button**: an announce step's label reads the message it will show (`announceLabel`): "Reveal the answer" for truth/bar-chart reveals, "Start the first round"/"Start the next round" for "Round N" intros, "Play the video", "Show the picture"; plain messages keep "Show the message"; four new rows in every language table; the Simple view's "+ Change the next button" override still wins. **Simulator first-run card** (`/prototype`): three lines (you play the whole class; Bot Fill answers for everyone; Skip timer moves on) shown once after the first Launch, remembered in localStorage, a `?` in the top bar brings it back. Tests 1579 → 1605.

- 2026-09-03 (the one-minute video rig): owner asked what it would take to make a compelling one-minute video from screen recordings and an AI voice, then said try it, with the Totem's fun vibe. New `scripts/video/` (README there): `stage.html` is a Totem-styled classroom (cream ground, paper scraps, a black-bordered projector frame and up to three Chromebook bezels, each an iframe onto the running server, plus lower-third captions, title cards and a fake cursor); `record.js` drives a headless Edge over CDP, plays Exquisite Corpse with one visibly-typing student (Priya), two quiet Chromebooks and nine socket bots, moves the camera between scenes (yard, Make it yours, projector, split, trio, solo), and captures every painted frame with `Page.startScreencast` plus named marks; `narrate.js` speaks `script.json` with OpenAI `gpt-4o-mini-tts` (voice coral, warm-teacher instructions); `assemble.js` turns the frames into a master, cuts one clip per beat to the voice (freeze-frame when the footage runs out, multi-shot beats for the fold-pass montage), and mixes optional music. Story: a finished six-hand sentence as the hook, the yard, Make it yours, the room code, students typing in, Start, the word rounds, the reveal on three Chromebooks, jamyard.xyz. First cut v3 (69s, no music) delivered to the owner. Findings along the way: the file:// stage makes the http iframes out-of-process targets (attach via Target.setAutoAttach, match by iframe name in each child's frame tree); Exquisite Corpse is not featured so it is invisible in the yard, the rig flips the flag on a second server (:3005) only while recording; submit-response is async through the moderation ladder, so a step closed 600ms after the last bot emit dropped words and the chain came back with holes ("wifi happens"), the director now waits for the projector's "12 of 12 submitted". Open: music track, owner's ear on voice and wording, trim toward 60s, YouTube upload and GUIDE_VIDEO_ID. Nothing in the app changed; tests untouched.
- 2026-09-03 (Sonnet 4.5 to Sonnet 5): owner's call after a pricing look. `MODELS.sonnet` is now `claude-sonnet-5` ($2/$10 per MTok vs $3/$15, and stronger); every judgment task (deep review, revise, quiz questions, bluff facts, suggestions, storyboard, matcher) rides it. Sonnet 5 changed the request surface, so `_callClaude` applies a per-model policy (`SONNET_POLICY`): adaptive thinking at medium effort (about Sonnet 4.6 at high, cheaper and faster than the high default) and a 16k max_tokens floor so thinking plus the answer fit under the new tokenizer (~30% more tokens for the same text; a cap only costs what is used). No call sent temperature/top_p/top_k or an assistant prefill, which now 400, and `extractText` already skipped non-text blocks. Haiku 4.5 stays for summarize/generate/triage. Smoke-tested live through the service: a direct call and generateQuizQuestions both clean (1.4s and 1.7s, zero thinking tokens on those easy asks). docs/AI-TASK-DESIGN.md model table refreshed. 1589 tests.
- 2026-09-03 (bold that looks bold): owner asked the design chat to bold some words and got "**word**" in the Designer, which read as code. The double stars were already the bold form the projector renders (shared/rich-text.js), but only for announce and reveal messages, the AI style rules forbade them, and the editor showed them raw. Now: (1) **Bold box** (`screens/shared/bold-box.js`, 12 tests): the Simple view's text fields and the token box's text segments are contenteditables that parse **word** into bold on load and write it back on every edit, so the config still holds plain text plus the one marker and nothing else (fonts, links, colors collapse to text on the way out; paste is plain). Ctrl/Cmd+B or the B chip in the corner toggles bold on the selection. The wrapper keeps a .value accessor so the mic badge dictates into it like a textarea (speech-input sweeps `[data-bold-box]`). Gotcha found live: the Simple view card is weight 600, so the browser saw every word as already bold and toggled OFF; the box pins weight 400. (2) **Every step prompt honors bold** on both screens: 27 textContent sinks in host.js/player.js (collect and choice prompts, rank/match/sort/checklist/buzz/estimate/rate/wager/relay/turn/merge instructions, solo-quiz question, done and end messages) go through `setRichText` -> `RichText.applyInline` (createElement/textContent only). Prompts are already set heavy, so bold shows as the Totem yellow paint band (`.prompt-bold`), the same mark as "The room is open." (3) **AI rules**: STYLE_RULES now names double stars as the one formatting mark (a word or short phrase, only when asked); the design chat is told bold lives inside the activity text, never in its replies. (4) Excerpts strip the markers (map rail, Simple-view gists). CDP-verified: bold renders in the editor box, real Ctrl+B and the chip bold and un-bold words with the config updated, projector and student prompt both paint the words. 1589 tests (12 new).
- 2026-09-02 (Make it yours, Simulate, and the three doors): owner's wording pass, two words retired from every teacher-facing string. **Customize** is now **Make it yours** (yard cards, the home carousel popup, the Create page's ready-made cards and its "find it in the yard" note, the plank aria-labels, the loading-state label; the yard's pile of your own copies is labeled "Yours"; the first-visit strip and the rescue line reword to match). **Preview** is now **Simulate** (yard and Create cards, the editor header button and its Simple-view block, the first-visit strip, the guide, the share page's follow-up link) and the /prototype page is titled **Simulator**. Left alone on purpose: the editor's Advanced "Customize screens" fold (custom HTML templates), its "Show Preview" live-mockup toggle, and the "Teacher preview" step type, all different things. **Three doors** close every Make it yours dialog (generic, quiz, bluff) in place of Make my copy / Skip / Set it up: "Continue setup in the designer", "See it in the simulator", "Host it now" (`makeItYoursDoors` + `COPY_DOORS` + `copyDestinationUrl` in library.js; `saveCopyAndReturn(config, dest)`). Rules: typed AI answers reword the copy first whichever door is picked; a touched knob or edited list saves the copy then goes; nothing chosen at all keeps the no-trace rule, the designer gets the unsaved draft and the simulator or host screen runs the ORIGINAL (an untouched copy would be the same activity under a second name). Equal weight, three paints (owner's follow-up the same day): the row is a three-column grid, every door the same painted-block style, cyan for the designer, magenta for the simulator, red for host (`.make-it-yours-doors .door-<dest>`), long labels wrap inside their block. CDP-verified: labels in all three dialogs, untouched Mood Check to /prototype and /host on the original id, Speed Quiz with a changed timer to a live host room on a fresh copy (deleted after). 1579 tests.

- 2026-09-02 (Make it yours text boxes grow with the entry): owner typed a long question into the Speed Quiz Customize dialog and could not see it, a single-line input scrolls the text off its right edge. New shared helper `screens/shared/growing-text.js` (`GrowingText.create({css, placeholder, maxLength, value})` + `fit`): a one-row textarea that resizes to its scrollHeight on every keystroke (grows and shrinks), stays one row while empty (overflow:clip + content-box clip margin so a wrapped placeholder never peeks through the bottom padding; hidden while measuring, since clip breaks the scrollHeight read), and keeps single-line semantics (Enter swallowed, pasted newlines become spaces). Every free-text field in the three dialogs uses it: quiz question + choices + topic, bluff fact + real answer + decoy + topic, and the generic AI-question answers; the data-role=question focus selector no longer assumes an input. Number fields untouched. CDP-verified in the Speed Quiz dialog: a two-line question and a two-line choice both show whole, the empty topic box is clean. 1579 tests (5 new).

- 2026-09-02 (report follow-up: the open step shows up): owner tried Exit Ticket and the report was empty. The report only read phaseData, and a collect step stores its responses at CLOSE, so a rolling exit ticket, read while answers are still arriving, had nothing. `buildActivityReport` now reads the LIVE room for the current step (`liveDataFor`): an open collect/collect-choice section is built from the players' responses (hidden and passed left out, choice tally computed), an unfinished solo quiz is graded on the fly from its mirrored progress, and the section carries a "Still open, this is what has come in so far" status line and `live: true`; stored data still wins once the step closes. Multi-field answers print one labeled line per question ("Learned: Fractions" / "Question: Why decimals?") instead of the stored "a | b" join, live and closed alike. Proven with a socket script (room, two students, fields submitted, report fetched mid-step: 200, one live section, both entries). 1574 tests (4 new).
- 2026-09-02 (rolling start: three activities for the first two minutes of class): owner's brainstorm became a primitive plus three recipes. (1) **The primitive**, top-level `start: "rolling"` (engine/phases/rolling.js; validator accepts together|rolling, warns on roster-bound steps and on timers): create-room transitions lobby -> first step at once (START_GAME ignores a room already past the lobby, so sim harnesses can't blow past it); collect/collect-choice ignore their timers; the host pins a **doorway card** (`#rolling-door`: painted code blocks, the address, a QR, a live head count) beside every section for the whole activity and drops it at the end; a student whose LAST input lands (`moreInputAhead` false on the main chain, vote counted as input) gets `player-done` and their own **done screen** (new `doneMessage` field on the screenControl mixin, translated when it is the default line) instead of the shared wait screen; reconnects honor it; the activity-run metric fires on first join instead of Start. Editor Settings gets a **Start** select (together | rolling), `carryStart` keeps it through AI edits. (2) **Exit Ticket** (recipe + featured built-in): one to three questions as a `$map`-built multi-field collect, an `anonymous` param, a `thanks` line; the projector shows counts only and answers are read on the console or the report. (3) **Live Poll**: new `collect-choice.liveResults` makes the projector draw the tally as answers land (`emitLiveTally` on submit and on moderate-hide, engine/phases/live-tally.js, counts only, ChartRender rows; the submission pile stays down); recipe + featured built-in on the class-poll prompt decks. (4) **Solo Quiz = phase type #30 `solo-quiz`** (engine/phase-handlers/solo-quiz.js + engine/phases/solo-quiz-scoring.js): each student walks a question list on their own device, right/wrong feedback (optionally with the answer) after each, their own score at the end; the projector shows a progress board only (started/working/finished + per-question correct rates, never a question or a name); two-stage close (End the quiz -> board with class average -> Continue; console gets 'End the quiz' via closeLabelFor); progress mirrored into phaseData so a restart resumes everyone; report builder (class average, per-question table, per-student table); editor name/blurb/palette/family/detail card; AI guidance + required fields; simulator + prototype Bot Fill play the whole quiz through; recipe reuses the quiz Customize panel (`setupPanel: "quiz"`) so questions are editable and AI-writable. i18n: 20 new strings across all five tables (done screen, quiz labels, 'Start the quiz'/'End the quiz'). Found and fixed in preview: feedback never reached students who joined after the step opened because the handler's onReconnect sent their question without a phaseInstanceId and the client's stale guard dropped it (rule recorded in CLAUDE.md gotchas). Proven headlessly on all three (Exit Ticket: door + fields + done screens + close; Live Poll: chart 2/2/0/0 as bots answered; Solo Quiz: Correct! feedback, four bots to their score screens, board 1/4 0/4 0/4 1/4 3/4, class average 25%, Continue to wrap-up), robot playtest clean on all three, carousel shots captured, editor renders the new step with Start = rolling. 1570 tests (25 new).

(1) **Design chat reads bigger**: panel 340 to 380px, messages/cards/hint up to 1rem, the ask box is four rows at 1rem with a 104px floor (feedback widget offset follows). (2) **Just do it**: a button above the ask box, shown once the AI has replied at least once and no proposal card is waiting (`ChatPanel.canJustDoIt`, pure + tested); it sends a fixed "Just do it." turn with `forceEdit:true`, and the server appends `JUST_DO_IT_INSTRUCTION` to the Haiku triage (choose "edit", fold every change discussed and not yet applied into ONE editRequest; only a conversation with no concrete change may answer back). Server-side continue/close labels translate at the one choke point (`continueLabelForPhase(phase, phases, lang)`; a teacher-typed continueLabel stays verbatim); `room-created`, `join-success` and `/api/rooms/:code/info` carry `{language, strings}`; new `screens/shared/ui-lang.js` swaps the static shell's exact-match text nodes and placeholders (originals remembered, so switching back to English is safe) and `UiLang.t()` covers the labels set from code. ~90 keys per language, tables drift-guarded for identical key sets. Not covered yet: dynamic status prose ("You matched 3 of 5"), the join instructions on the projector, the teacher console.
