- 2026-08-31 (the chain brick: pass-around mechanics reach the Create page): the storyboard vocabulary gains its first multi-phase MECHANIC brick beyond quiz — `chain` (screens/shared/step-suggestions.js appendPassChain): the AI supplies only `start`, 1-6 per-hop `hops` instructions, a `visibility` choice (all | tail | blind) and an optional blind-only `sentence` slot template; the compiler emits every rotateFrom link, prefill/appendOnly/showTail flag, and the return-to-author reveal deterministically (blind prompts get {{...}} tokens stripped with a problem note — the fold is structural). Concierge cleaner passes the new fields through trimmed (engine/suggest-validate.js); both AI prompts (storyboard generator + concierge) teach the brick, and the generator's honesty rule now names chains as the one legal pass-around (never fake one from a row of collects). Gated exactly as planned: eval-designer-prompts.js grew a storyboard-generator leg (Sonnet per storyboard/cantBuild corpus entry: generate → compileStoryboard → validate), baseline run BEFORE the prompt edits showed hypothesis-relay refused with "can't pass one student's answer to specific other students" — the after run builds it as announce → chain → end, hostable, 3/3 on the leg; matcher leg unchanged except recipe-vs-built-in twin wobble (whitelisted via acceptAlso; vocab-riddles matcher finding still parked). Smoke calls show the generator choosing tail for folded stories and blind+sentence with the right slot count for one-word poems, both compiling clean. New corpus entry hypothesis-relay (per-hop-different instructions, the case no fixed recipe covers). 1499 tests.
- 2026-08-30 (Exquisite Corpse + the fold + golden-prompt harness): the owner's "exquisite corpse" prompt failed in Create even though the engine already had 90% of the mechanic (chain-reveal.js names the payoff in its own docstring) — the storyboard brick vocabulary simply has no chain brick. Shipped the reliability-first slice of the fix, riskiest piece deferred: (1) reveal `chainDisplay:"template"` + `chainTemplate` assembles blind one-word chain contributions into a slotted sentence ("The {1} {2}...", missing slots render as blanks; validator + editor mirror reject a template with no {N} slots); (2) recipe #23 **exquisite-corpse** (blind six-word grammar chain, theme + wordTimer knobs, zero AI calls, connection family) with a recipe-born, provenance-stamped, drift-guarded built-in `games/exquisite-corpse` — blindness is free because `{{X.assigned}}` was always opt-in; (3) **collect.showTail** (the fold): on an appendOnly chain, players see only the last N words of the inherited text behind an ellipsis while the full text accumulates server-side (tailOfWords in engine/phases/append-only.js; masking is server-side so devtools can't peek; validator requires appendOnly — an editable prefill would amputate the artifact on submit — and warns if the prompt shows `{{.assigned}}` anyway; `_sim-fold` fixture proves it on the wire); (4) **golden-prompt harness**: tests/designer/golden-prompts.json (12 teacher asks) with a CI test proving every expected deliverable compiles/validates deterministically, plus scripts/eval-designer-prompts.js running the corpus through the live matcher (baseline 9/12 + 2 defensible near-matches whitelisted via acceptAlso; the exquisite-corpse prompt now one-shots to the new recipe). Baseline finding, parked: on vocab-riddles the matcher fakes a weak recipe fit (creative-vote) instead of returning noMatch to hand off to the storyboard generator. Deferred by design: the `chain` storyboard brick (a matcher/storyboard prompt change — make it only with a before/after eval run). New sims: simulate-exquisite-corpse.js (blindness at the transport level, return-to-author, whole assembly + the fold run; submit waits ride `response-received` acks because submit-response is async since the moderation ladder). 1487 tests.

- 2026-08-30 (the variety spin: AI questions stop repeating across sessions): owner previewed Trivia Bluff live mode and got the same questions every time — the 2026-08-16 lesson (identical generate instructions = identical facts) striking ACROSS sessions: the compiled instruction is byte-identical every game, and Haiku converges hard (two baseline runs produced the same three facts, muscle/Lesotho/flamingos, verbatim). Fix at the call boundary so recipes and drift-guarded configs stay untouched: ai-process appends `varietySpin()` (engine/phases/variety-spin.js, pure + 7 tests) to every `generate` task instruction. Built empirically in three iterations, each live-tested with real Haiku: (1) a random spin number + brainstorm-N-take-the-last maneuver alone moved only the free-pick round (Lesotho and flamingos held); (2) a random INSPIRATION WORD from a 60-noun school-safe bank broke the category gravity wells (zero overlap between runs) but pulled round 1 toward etymology facts every time (a word seed associates to word origins) — countered with "associate through THINGS, not language"; (3) the model's observed chestnut attractors still resurfaced ~1/3 of the time, so the spin now bans them by name (Lesotho/San Marino, flamingo-group names, honey, octopus hearts, banana berries, Eiffel growth, wombat cubes, Oymyakon). Final proof: consecutive live runs share zero questions; the last four runs produced 12 distinct facts (volcano, Bhutan's GDP measure, woodpecker tongues, monsoon, Moldova, ferrets...). Transform tasks (summarize/compare/judge) vary naturally with student input and are left unspun. 1454 tests (7 new).
- 2026-08-30 (the shared meadow: everyone's in the same space): the owner's follow-up on the meadow — sync block colors and locations between players "so it's like everyone's in the same space together." The base layout was ALREADY identical everywhere (deterministic index tones + golden-angle scatter), so the build was agreeing on identity and relaying movement. Server (`engine/meadow-sync.js`, 9 tests): every submitter gets a canonical block index in submission order, assigned once per phase (`meadowIndexFor`, state resets with phaseInstanceId; reconnects covered by adding `room.meadowState` to the id-migration walk); told privately via new `meadow-you` on the collect and vote submit paths. Nudges: new `meadow-nudge` (payload-schema'd, stale-guarded, submitters-only, server-enforced 3s cooldown, coordinates clamped; deliberately NOT journaled — high-frequency cosmetic garnish would flush real events) relays room-wide as `meadow-moved {index, fx, fy}` — normalized field fractions so a narrow Chromebook and a wide screen agree, and NEVER a name or player id (counts-only doctrine holds; a student wiggling to say "that's me" is self-disclosure, the system attributes nothing). Client (meadow.js rework): blocks are canonical index-keyed everywhere now (previously YOUR block always sat at spot 0 — screens showed different rooms), your server-told index wears the you-paint, moves are remembered per index across re-renders, the generic waiting screen stays watch-only but follows the shared moves, and your own nudge applies optimistically before the echo. Proven three ways: scripts/simulate-meadow-sync.js (8 checks: order, private indexes, room-wide anonymous payload with exactly index+fx+fy, cooldown eat + re-arm, clamping, non-submitters can't move), simulate-holding regression (counts-only invariant intact), and a two-headless-tab browser run where a click in tab A walked the right block in tab B (screenshot). Anti-rush stance unchanged: same cooldown, nothing accumulates, the {you:false} dial remains. 1447 tests (13 new).
- 2026-08-30 (the finale leaderboard cheers): owner played Speed Quiz and saw only confetti at the end — Speed Quiz has NO winner phase (its big moment is a leaderboard into end), so the meadow cheer never had a place to fire; the confetti seen was the #1 player's own screen celebrating. The leaderboard handler now computes `final` (next phase is `end`, or no next; foreach-inner boards can't qualify since virtual ids resolve no config phase) and sends it to the host, which throws `J.cheer()` on the finale board only — mid-game boards stay tada-quiet so the cheer can't wear out. Proven by handler unit tests (3, via a minimal ctx against the registered handler) + a live speed-quiz sim logging ", final". 1434 tests.
- 2026-08-30 (graded bar charts: green means CORRECT, not popular): owner field report from Speed Quiz — the answer-reveal announce colors the biggest bar green, and green reads as "right", so whenever the class's top pick was wrong the chart contradicted the "The answer was: X!" line above it. Fix across the text contract and the renderer: `formatBarChart` (engine/game-engine.js) now takes the phase's stored RESOLVED `correctAnswer` (written into phaseData at close by the grading path, so it works inside foreach rounds too; bare `{{X.barChart}}` only, deeper paths chart arbitrary data) and marks that row with a trailing ✓, matched exactly like scoring matches (trim + lowercase); if NOBODY picked the correct answer it gets an appended `0 (0%)` row, the most confusing case of all. `chart-render.js` parses the ✓ (optional in CHART_LINE), and on a graded chart colors ONLY the correct row green (green fill + bold label + green ✓ chip; host + player CSS) while the most-picked row goes neutral; ungraded charts (polls, votes) keep the old top-row accent, where "green = the winner" is honest. Screenshot-proven on the exact failure case (London 71% neutral, Paris 14% green ✓) + clean speed-quiz sim. 1431 tests (4 new).
- 2026-08-30 (celebrations wear the theme: torn paper + the meadow cheer): the confetti was 120 drifting rectangles that read as winter snowfall on the gesso ground (owner's call from a three-option brainstorm). Two replacements in juice.js, same canvas-overlay rules as before (self-cleaning, reduced-motion skips, garnish never breaks the game). (1) **Torn-paper scraps everywhere**: every confetti piece is now an irregular 5-7 sided torn polygon (`scrapPolygon`, pure + exported + tested); the default palette became Totem paints + paper cream, and paper-cream scraps carry a stray pencil line plus a whisper of an edge, like bits of someone's worksheet. `confettiColors()` now drops ink-dark theme values (`--theme-heading`/`--theme-button` are often the text color; 40% of scraps rendered as black dirt specks in the first screenshot) and tops every mix up with paint yellow + paper cream. All existing host/player confetti call sites inherit the look with zero changes. (2) **The meadow cheer** (`Juice.cheer`), the winner moment's signature, wired at the host winner reveal in place of plain confetti: the anonymous painted blocks with ink legs from the waiting meadow (canvas twins of meadow.css's skewed-quad body, nine-tone palette, meadow proportions) run in from both edges along the bottom of the projector on staggered golden-ratio marks, waddle-bob while walking, then hop in place and toss little torn scraps at each hop, fading with the same envelope. The class itself celebrates the winner. Screenshot-verified both mid-animation over CDP. 1427 tests (4 new).
- 2026-08-30 (the moderation ladder: meaning, not just words): the blocklist catches profanity; it cannot catch "nobody would miss you" or an unkind note in a Someone's Got You round. The owner's three-rung design ships in `services/moderation-ladder.js`: (1) **OpenAI's free moderation endpoint** (omni-moderation-latest) scores every free-text collect submission and relay line; any category at or above `MODERATION_BLOCK_AT` (default 0.85) rejects with a friendly "That message can't go to the class. Reword it and try again." (2) The **uncertain band** (0.4 to 0.85) escalates to **Haiku** (`AIService.moderateText`, budget-gated, injection-hardened data-not-instructions prompt, regex verdict extraction per the standing gotcha) which has the classroom context a moderation model lacks: kid banter and game trash-talk pass, cruelty in clean language blocks. (3) Haiku's "unsure" (parse failures and budget 429s included) **flags the entry on the teacher console**: `responseFlagged` on the player rides `buildSubmissionList` as `flagged` into the console's live entries list, drawn as a yellow "Needs a look" chip next to the existing Hide/Kick buttons; teacher surfaces only, the projector never sees a flag. Failure directions are deliberate: OpenAI unreachable fails OPEN (blocklist already passed, a dead third party must not stop a class); Haiku unreachable fails TO THE TEACHER. Scope per the owner: collect submits + relay lines; NOT merge drafts (per-keystroke would lag the shared pen; the blocklist still runs there) and NOT drawings (teacher preview stays the control). Wiring: submit-response went async with the staleness guard + player lookup RE-RUN after the await (the phase can close mid-check); relay re-checks kind/stale/turn the same way; simulated rooms skip the ladder; text is PII-scrubbed (scrubForAI) before anything leaves the server; per-IP-free (OpenAI moderation is free and doesn't count toward usage). Off entirely without `OPENAI_API_KEY` (render.yaml documents it, sync: false); `OPENAI_MODERATION_URL` override enables stub testing. /privacy discloses OpenAI as a processor (no-training note included). Proven live against a local score stub with REAL Haiku on rung 2: 0.95 harassment rejected at the OpenAI rung in 26ms, 0.55 "nobody in this class likes you" rejected at the Haiku rung, 0.55 "pizza is the best school lunch" cleared by Haiku, clean text stored untouched; fail-open proven with a bogus key (157ms accept). 1423 tests (21 new).
- 2026-08-30 (the sharing system: share links that import a copy): the last queued item from the owner feedback batch. A teacher's activity can now travel to a colleague as a plain link, `jamyard.xyz/share/<id>`, no accounts and no minted short-code table needed (user game ids are already readable slugs, and configs are already public by id — sharing was a UX + copy-semantics problem, exactly as the design notes said). Semantics are copy-import, never the row: (1) **`POST /api/games/:gameId/copy`** loads the source, strips `featured` (a copy never arrives pre-starred), mints a deduped id (source id, else -2, -3... across built-ins + filesystem + DB via an async exists callback), validates, and saves a fresh user game; pure logic in `engine/share-copy.js` (mintCopyId + prepareSharedCopy, 9 tests), per-IP rate-limited (10/min, the feedback limiter pattern) because every call writes a row. Safe to share by construction: teacher-save purity means saved configs never contain student data. (2) **`GET /share/:gameId`** serves the new landing page (`screens/share/index.html`, Totem paper sheet): "A teacher shared an activity with you", name + description, the treasure map (shared ActivityMap), a red "Save to my activities" button that calls the copy endpoint, `MyGames.add`s the new id, and flips to "is in your yard now" with Open-the-yard/Preview doors; "Try a preview first" links /prototype on the source id; a localStorage import memory (`lanyard-share-imported`) makes revisits say "You already saved this one" with a quieter "Save another copy". Built-in ids redirect to `/library?about=<id>` (they already have a public home); dead links get an honest "isn't here anymore" page (the page fetches the config itself, so the route stays dumb); `share` added to VANITY_RESERVED. Everything renders through textContent (name/description are teacher content, untrusted). (3) **Share button in the yard popup** (own activities only, next to the shed): copies the link, flashes "Link copied!", clipboard fallback ladder from the host screen. Verified end to end: API smoke (copy minted `superhero-spectrum-2`, featured stripped, built-in redirect 302, missing id 404) + CDP walkthrough screenshots (landing page, saved state, popup with SHARE, revisit state — the one revisit miss was the known headless-Edge localStorage denial, and the page's try/catch handled it as designed). Copies survive an eventual accounts migration by being ordinary user games. 1402 tests (9 new).
- 2026-08-30 (owner feedback batch, part 6, polish round): (1) checklist task editor hardened: always the role-aware editor, so `{text, role}` items render as their text even with no Assign Roles step in the plan (the plain editor showed them as "[object Object]"; hidden tags survive and revive if a roles step is added, a fact hints at it). (2) Preview grew a persistent **✎ Edit** button in the control bar (→ /designer/edit for the selected activity, follows the picker), so the designer is one click away from any door. (3) One-at-a-time carousel arrows + dots re-anchored from the viewport to the stage row with rail-aware math (`--rail-w` via :has) — the left arrow sat over the TEACHER screen once the map rail shifted the layout; now it hugs the player panel's left edge. (4) Back links say **yard**: editor "Back to the yard", create page "The yard" + "Open the yard", preview "The yard" (owner's naming call for the teacher-facing word; route stays /library). (5) Host roster planks drop the painted-initial chip, the name alone is the block (nameToColor retired). (6) **Empty role list = "no roles", legally**: the editor has no delete-a-step affordance, so clearing every role box is how a teacher opts out — the validator now warns (skip) instead of erroring (which blocked save and therefore Preview), the card says "No roles yet, so this step is skipped", and the handler's runtime skip is proven by the games/_sim-roles-skip fixture (empty roles step self-skips into the checklist; orphaned item tags render as labels, nobody assigned). **group-work setup knobs on the Simple-view cards** (owner: "needs more in the setup window"). Split into Teams card un-muted and grew real controls: SIZING (groups of N / exactly N teams, mutually exclusive writes) + HOW (random / students pick / you arrange; balanced shown only when already set, it needs a score ref). Assign Roles card: HOW select (students pick / dealt at random) beside the role list. Group Checklist card: when the plan has an Assign Roles step, every task gets a "job:" dropdown (everyone / role names from that step) — all "everyone" = one shared list, tag some = role jobs, tag all = each role its own list; tagging syncs `rolesFrom` automatically and untagging all clears it. Items stay plain strings until tagged so old configs round-trip byte-exact. New shared card helpers `svSelect`/`svControlRow` + narrow-card two-line task blocks (the mic wrapper + role select don't fit one line).
- 2026-08-30 (owner feedback batch, part 4): **recipe maps + team roles.** (1) Recipes draw the same what-happens treasure map as activities: `POST /api/recipes/:id/compile` and `/api/games/from-description` responses carry `map` (buildActivityMap of the compiled config), new `GET /api/recipes/:id/map` compiles with parameter defaults (400 when a recipe has none, clients skip quietly), and the designer renders the trail in the recipe picker form + AI match preview (`appendRecipeMap`). (2) **Phase type #29: `team-roles`** — every group member gets a job. `teamsFrom` (team-split or pairwise collect via the pairs bridge, whole-class fallback), `roles` list, `method` random (round-robin deal after shuffle, pure logic in engine/phases/role-deal.js) or choice (students claim; per-group capacity ceil(members/roles), re-picks move, full roles bounce with truth re-sent, all-picked auto-closes, the team-split confirm button doubles as the straggler sweep; closeTeamRoles kind-guarded + idempotent + reachable from advance-phase). Output byPlayer means `{{step.mine}}` = your role; `rolesList` = the per-group lineup. Host/player screens reuse the team-split boards. (3) **Checklist role tags**: `rolesFrom` + items as `{text, role}` objects — tagged items show whose job they are, the viewer's own wear "your job" in yellow; anyone can still check anything (trust model unchanged). (4) **Group Work Day** now runs split → pick your role → role-tagged checklist. Proven by scripts/simulate-team-roles.js (menus, collision bounce, distinct roles per group, checklist knows each student's role, full run) + a clean robot playtest; 29 new tests (1393).
- 2026-08-30 (owner feedback batch, part 3): **the preview map rail** — /prototype grew a left "THE PLAN" rail drawing the same treasure map as the library popups, with a yellow "you are here" mark that follows the live room (owner's ask: know what you're seeing and where you're going while previewing). Mechanics: `buildActivityMap` stops now carry the phase ids they cover (`ids`, 3 new tests); the host iframe's prototype postMessage hands over the teacher PIN (same-origin targetOrigin now, not '*'), and the preview page pairs a silent teacher-console socket to receive teacher-phase events — zero new server events. Highlight walks start chip → stops (rounds stops match any covered id; unmatched inner-round ids keep the last mark) → wrap-up X. Host's "Teacher device connected" chip suppressed in prototype mode (the rail's pairing would ghost-announce on every launch). Proven live via CDP: lobby → announce → folded-rounds stop all tracked. Rail hides under 900px and on Reset.
- 2026-08-30 (owner feedback batch, part 2): **picture/video settings return to the editor**, URL + YouTube only (owner's call: no file uploads, so nothing lands on the deploy-ephemeral disk). `mediaEditor()` in simple-view.js: a quiet "+ Add a picture or video" on the Simple-view detail card for announce/collect/collect-choice/reveal (estimate gets picture-only), expanding to a Picture address row with a live thumbnail (proof the URL loads) and a YouTube row with a soft non-YouTube warning + "plays on the projector, not student devices" note. Writes the existing schema `image`/`video` fields; host/player display paths were already shipped (applyImage/applyVideo), so this is purely un-hiding authoring. The upload widget + `POST /api/games/:id/assets` remain parked behind the disabled All-settings surface.
- 2026-08-30 (owner feedback batch, part 1): (1) **`rotateShuffle` shuffled deal** — new boolean on rotating collects (`engine/phases/deal.js` shuffleDeal: Fisher-Yates into one circle, so every player gets exactly one classmate's item, never their own, who-got-whose unpredictable; plain rotateFrom stays the fixed join-order shift). `PER_PLAYER_TOKEN` moved to `engine/resolver-grammar.js` and now matches `.assigned` as well as `.mine`, so announce/reveal can show each student their dealt item (previously only collect prompts could). Creator brick docs teach the shuffled deal + the several-pools chain pattern (each step's rotateFrom deals the PREVIOUS pool) — the missing knowledge that sank the owner's creative-writing attempt. (2) **Story Ingredients** (game #36, `games/story-ingredients/`): everyone invents a character/setting/twist, pools shuffle-dealt back out, timed story writing from the dealt hand, host-paced share; `scripts/simulate-story-ingredients.js` proves complete no-self permutation deals on all three pools. 12 new tests (1364). (3) **Caret visible again**: `caret-color: var(--t-ink)` on all inputs in totem.css (wood-grain planks ate the 1px caret) and the Create page idea box no longer rotates (a rotated textarea antialiases the caret invisible; body text never rotates). (4) **Textarea scrollbars themed**: standard `scrollbar-width/scrollbar-color` (thin, wood-tone thumb, no arrow buttons) replacing raw gray OS chrome. (5) **Homepage totem labels spelled out**: Pick an activity / Create an activity / Enter a room code / Guide. (6) **/privacy operator facts filled** (Max Cady, scraper-proof written-out email); local dev split onto a Neon dev branch (ops, no code). Queued with findings: image/video editor resurfacing, preview map rail, recipe maps, Group Work Day roles, sharing — see NEXT-STEPS "Owner feedback batch 2026-08-30".

- 2026-08-27 (the waiting meadow + yard organization): two ships from the owner's brainstorm about what students do after submitting (and what a cluttered yard needs). (1) **The meadow** (`screens/shared/meadow.js` + `.css`, 15 unit tests on the pure geometry): the player wait screens grow a little field where every submitted classmate is an anonymous painted block with ink legs that walks in from the edge as the count rises — same nine-tone paint language as the host's submission pile. Your own block (yellow, ink outline, "you" tag) can be nudged a few steps by tapping the field; bumping a classmate is a cosmetic wobble for both. Anti-rush rules are structural, per the owner's worry that a fun wait screen teaches rushing: nothing accumulates, nothing completes, no sound, a 3s nudge cooldown so mashing does nothing, the field only exists after you submit, and being FIRST in is the dullest seat (an empty meadow) — the interesting event is classmates arriving, which rushing can't speed up. Deterministic golden-angle scatter so re-renders never reshuffle; prefers-reduced-motion drops all animation. Mounted in #submitted-section, #vote-submitted-section, and #game-waiting-section (that one watch-only, `{you:false}` — non-eligible players share the screen so "you" might not be counted). Server side: rank/sort/match/rate/wager/estimate submit handlers now broadcast the same counts-only `room-progress {count, total}` collect and vote always did (the never-names invariant holds; simulate-holding grew a rank leg proving payload keys are exactly count,total), and #game-waiting-section gained the "N of M in" line. showSection resets stale meadows on entering a wait screen fresh; the phase's own first tick repopulates within a beat. CSS gotcha found by screenshot: clip-path on the block clipped the pseudo-element legs — body paint moved to ::before so the legs survive. (2) **Yard organization**: one ordering rule for the My yard row via pure `ActivityPrefs.orderYard` — hearted first (♥ now doubles as "keep this plank up front"), then recently used (MRU), then the rest newest-saved first (reverse MyGames order: no timestamps needed, works in DB and filesystem modes). Plus **the shed**: `ActivityPrefs.Archived` (`lanyard-archived`, device-local like MyGames) — "Put in the shed" in the activity popup (the safe cousin of Delete, own-copies only), a collapsed "▸ In the shed (N)" line under the yard board expanding to a dimmed plank row, and shed items stay searchable (a live search puts them back on the board so nothing is ever lost). Placement loop also dedupes repeated /api/games rows (found live: elimination-tournament is served twice, disk games/user copy + Neon row — data cleanup queued in NEXT-STEPS). Shed line moved outside .myyard because the board's hard drop-shadow filter ghosted the toggle text (misprint effect). 9 activity-prefs tests; screenshot-verified meadow, yard row, shed open/closed. 1352 tests.

- 2026-08-27 (map round 3, the Closer test: folded runs show their content): owner clicked Closer and still didn't get it — its map read "13 rounds: Announcement", which describes plumbing, not an activity whose entire substance is the CONTENT of those 13 screens. Two general fixes. (1) **Folded runs sample excerpts from across the run** (`samples`, up to 3: first, middle, last, then any others, deduped) instead of keeping only the opener — Closer now shows "This isn't a quiz. No points, no winners, and nothing to type…" / "What's something you've changed your mind about in the last…" / "What's a moment from this year you want to remember in ten…", and quiz runs show a spread of their actual questions. Renderer renders each as its own quoted line; foreach keeps its single `detail`. (2) **Talk-driven activities say so**: root `talk: true` when the walked path has zero typed-input phases (collect/collect-two/relay/merge) AND 3+ announces making up at least half the stops — rendered as a rider right under the start chip: "nothing to type: the screen asks, the class talks". Also: single-type folds drop the "rounds" framing ("13 × Announcement", not "13 rounds") since a run of talk screens isn't game rounds; renderer's render() now takes the whole map (attach updated). Closer's popup verified: description → talk note → 13 × Announcement with three real conversation questions → one-tap checkout → X. 1327 tests (3 new).
- 2026-08-27 (trail riders: the map shows students working off each other): owner's follow-up on the treasure map — "it's not conveying one of Jamyard's comparative advantages, students working off each other's thoughts and ideas." The map now puts the CARGO on the trail: wherever student work travels between steps, a small tilted paper note rides the dashed line, worded in the student's voice. Engine: `carriesFor(phase, phases)` in activity-map.js derives a semantic key per stop from the config's own refs — collect `rotateFrom` → classmate-work; reveal `scope:"pair"` → partner-swap; reveal `scope:"own"`+chainFrom → back-to-author; collect-choice `choicePool[].from` into student work → answers-become-choices; vote/rank candidates ref or `matchupsFromPairs` → class-judges-own; ai-process/ai-eliminate `input` ref → ai-reads-pile; reveal content/from refs AND template-embedded `{{refs}}` (snowball's reveal is a template, caught in verification) → work-goes-up-front (announce `drawingFrom` too); merge → partners-combine; relay → build-on-last; foreach `data` ref → round-per-answer. Ref detection checks the SOURCE phase's type against a student-material set (collects, relay, merge, rank/rate/sort/match, buzz, wager, plus ai-process as derived material), so a vote over teacher-fixed options stays unlabeled. Folded rounds carry the first hand-off found inside the block (trivia-bluff's round shows "classmates' answers become the choices"). Renderer: `CARRY_COPY` key→copy map in shared/activity-map.js (unknown keys draw nothing, so keys can ship server-first), `.amap-rider` notes in the CSS. Coverage check across all 41 built-ins: 30 show at least one hand-off; the 11 without are honestly the quiz/solo/talk shapes (speed-quiz, vocab-match, closer, one-voice) — the differentiation IS the feature. Verified: snowball reads collect → "partners combine their answers into one" → merge → "everyone's work goes up on the projector" → reveal; dream-vacation shows five riders. 1324 tests (8 new).
- 2026-08-27 (the activity treasure map): the owner's big observation-wave ask ships — "someone should understand what happens in an activity without reading through it or playing the whole thing in preview... inspired by a treasure map, but stick with the theme." Both activity popups (library plank popup AND the home carousel popup) now draw the activity's path: a pencil-dashed trail (the join input's dashed-line idiom) from a sanded "Everyone joins" chip, through painted number blocks wearing the step-family colors (same coding as the editor's stack: ask yellow / show green / decide magenta / team orange / AI cyan, rounds on walnut), down to a drawn red X at the wrap-up. Each stop shows the canonical step name (phase-names.js) plus a short quoted excerpt of the step's own words. Engine: new pure `engine/activity-map.js` `buildActivityMap(config)` (13 tests) — walks the primary path (next → approveNext → first nextByWinner branch; branching votes get a "the class's pick decides the path (N doors)" note), drops lobby/end (the renderer draws its own start and treasure marks), folds a foreach into one "N rounds" stop (sub-steps in their own next order; no limit = "one per answer"), and folds REPEATED COMPILED RUNS the same way — period up to 6 with a rotation-ambiguity guard preferring the fold that reaches the run's end, so trivia-bluff renders as intro → "3 rounds: AI → Announcement → Open answer → Multiple choice → Announcement" → leaderboard instead of 16 stops, and speed-quiz as announce → "5 rounds" → leaderboard. Excerpts skip {{template}} text and cut at 64 chars on a word boundary. Served by new public `GET /api/games/:gameId/map` (activity structure only, never student data). Renderer: shared `screens/shared/activity-map.js` + `activity-map.css`, everything through textContent (step details are teacher config, untrusted); arrives async into a holder above the popup's action row so buttons never jump; popup works fine if the fetch fails. Home dialog gained max-height + scroll for tall maps. Spot-checked five varied games' maps (snowball, closer, speed-quiz, one-voice, doodle-bluff) + screenshots of both popups. 1316 tests (13 new).
- 2026-08-27 (observation wave, round 2: four follow-ups): (1) **Emoji avatars off player names, everywhere for now** (owner: "they look like emojis and then in the game you don't see them"): every `J.avatarFor(name) + ' '` prefix removed — player screen ("Playing as", lobby roster chips), host joining roster (the painted chip keeps the name's color but shows the initial letter now), and the mid-game surfaces too so the inconsistency doesn't just flip around (leaderboards, team chips/cards, buzz, estimate, match/sort results, checklist labels, winner + standings). `Juice.avatarFor` itself stays (tested, harmless) for an easy rethink later; the winner's 👑 stays, it marks the moment, not a player. (2) **Library "New here? Start with this" card simplified into icon rows** like the plan-intro dialog owner liked: the three-bullet wall became three drawn-mark rows (green projector board "You project it up front", yellow room-code blocks "Students join with a room code. No accounts", cyan play wedge "Not sure? Preview one first, no class needed"); the 3-minutes line cut; guide link + class profile chips unchanged. (3) **Hover card's "Click for options" hint removed** (it read as a thing to literally click). (4) **The carousel popup opens right on the home page** — no detour through the yard: home loads shared/dialog.js and builds its own paper popup (magenta name chip, meta, description, red CUSTOMIZE → `?customize=`, quiet "See all activities →"), carousel rotation pauses while it's open and resumes on close; the slide keeps its `/library?about=` href so modified clicks/new tabs still land on the library popup. Screenshot-verified home popup, library card, emoji-free prototype lobby. 1303 tests.
- 2026-08-27 (observation wave: first-contact clarity, six fixes): owner watched real people use the site; the small findings shipped same-day, the big ones (activity map/path view, guide visuals, yard-vs-library naming, preview player-count default) are queued in NEXT-STEPS with rationale. (1) **Home carousel click opens the activity popup, not Customize**: the slide now points at new deep link `/library?about=<id>` (handleAboutDeepLink → openActivityDialog, param stripped like its siblings); clicking an activity you know nothing about was landing people in a Customize form before they knew what the thing was. The card's CUSTOMIZE button keeps the direct `?customize=` door. (2) **Home FIND block reads "Pick an activity"** (owner's wording; .act-find drops to 16px so three words sit on one block face). (3) **Yard planks grew hover cards**: rest the mouse on a plank (or mini plank) and one shared floating paper scrap shows name, time/no-winners meta, full description, and a "Click for options" hint; 220ms delay so sweeping the yard doesn't flicker, position clamped to the window (flips above near the bottom edge), pointer-events:none, hidden on click/refreshLibrary; native title removed from planks (would double up); pointer-only on purpose, keyboard/touch get the same words in the popup. (4) **Plan-intro dialog cut from four paragraphs to three icon rows** (key bumped to `lanyardPlanIntroSeen3`): "Each card is one step / Want bigger changes? Tell the chat / Then press Preview", each with a drawn CSS mark (step planks on birch, cyan speech bubble, play wedge on yellow) — observed teachers read v2 as a skippable wall and hit "Got it" blind. Close still spotlights the chat panel. (5) **Big Preview block under the editor's step stack**: a yellow painted block ("▶ Preview / play it with pretend students") ends the stack — the plan reads top to bottom and lands on the natural next act; calls the same testGame() as the unseen header button (red stays reserved for ▶ Host). drop-shadow not box-shadow (clip-path gotcha). (6) **Preview mode defaults flipped to first-timer shape**: one-at-a-time view is the default (grid stays one click away; the chosen view now survives Reset/relaunch instead of snapping back to grid), player count default 2 → 4 (owner floated 8; went 4 because one-at-a-time hides the extra screens anyway, pairs/teams still compose, and 9 iframes strain Chromebooks — revisit note in NEXT-STEPS), and **Bot Fill + Skip timer moved out of the crowded top bar into a bottom bench bar** — big cyan/yellow blocks with plain-words sublabels ("answers for everyone" / "move things along") and a "You play all the students" hint, sitting under the screens where the eyes actually are; in flow (iframes shrink a little) so nothing gets covered, carousel dots raised to clear it. Screenshot-verified home, library popup via ?about=, editor (dialog + preview block), running preview bench. 1303 tests.
- 2026-08-26 (phase-interop wave 4: small closers — no self-authored pair prompts, checklist takes pairs): the review's two remaining small items. (1) **pairsFrom author exclusion**: a pairwise collect drawing per-pair prompts from an earlier step's responses could hand a pair the very question one of its own members wrote (`items[gi % len]` ignored authorship). New pure `assignPromptsToGroups` (pairing.js): per group, prefer an unused item not authored by a member, then any non-member item (reuse when groups outnumber items), then the old rotation fallback (only when every item is member-authored); collect keeps item authorship through the mapping now. (2) **checklist accepts pairs** (bridge C): `teamsFrom` may name a pairwise collect — its pairs become dashboard groups labeled by member names ("Maya & Sam", collisions numbered) via new pure `pairsAsTeams` (checklist-state.js); validator allows it (+ warning when the source's oddHandling is sit-out, the benched player would get no checklist), editor picker offers "Pairs from ..." and mirrors the check, schema helper + AI guidance updated. Deliberately skipped: roadmap item #3 (reveal inside foreach) — a broadcast reveal in a round duplicates announce, and the valuable version (pair-private per round) needs the in-round pairing wave 1 banned; parked pending an owner call. Proof: `_sim-groups-bridge` grew a checklist leg (every pair shares one list labeled with both names) — full chain team-split → pairwise → pair reveal → merge → checklist 0 errors; simulate-connection-slice regression clean (pairsFrom distribution changed). 1303 tests (12 new: pair-prompts-and-checklist.test.js).
- 2026-08-26 (phase-interop wave 3: pairs/teams bridges): the three grouping systems (pairwise-collect `pairs`, team-split `teams`, merge's internal groups) get their two highest-value bridges, closing the review's finding that "the one grouping a teacher deliberately curates is the one nothing consumes". (1) **collect `reusePairsFrom` accepts a team-split**: a pairwise collect can adopt a team split's groups as its pairs, so teacher-arranged pairs (method "teacher", groupSize 2) and student-chosen partners (method "choice") now feed the whole pair pipeline (pair-private reveals, head-to-head matchups, same/new-partner chains) — normalized through new shared `groupsFromSource()` in engine/phases/pairing.js (reads `pairs` OR `teams` into plain id groups); rotatePairsFrom stays pairwise-only (its avoid-set semantics). Editor's pairing dropdown offers earlier team-splits as "Use the groups from ...". (2) **merge gains `groupsFrom`** (pairwise collect or team-split): the merge adopts exactly those groups instead of shuffling fresh ones — "same partners now write together" (think-pair-share continuity, teacher-arranged co-writing); conflicts with an explicit `groupSize` (validator-rejected), path-dominance checked (phaseAlwaysPrecedes), missing data degrades to normal grouping with a warn; `buildMergeGroups` grew a prebuilt-idGroups path (per-member seeds, ineligible members dropped, emptied groups vanish). Validator + editor mirror + Simple-view facts ("same groups as <step>") + AI guidance (new merge entry teaching both bridges). End-to-end proof: `games/_sim-groups-bridge` + `scripts/simulate-groups-bridge.js` — one grouping flows team-split → pairwise collect → pair reveal → merge intact (every reveal partner IS the teammate, every merge group IS the pair, merge auto-advances on all-submitted), 0 errors live. 1291 tests (12 new: pairs-teams-bridge.test.js).
- 2026-08-26 (phase-interop wave 2: pairBy, answer-keyed pairing): the missing brick from the morning's interop review ships. A pairwise collect can now prefer partners by what students answered in an earlier Multiple Choice step: `pairBy: {from: "<collect-choice id>", mode: "opposite" | "same"}` ("opposite" = share your why with someone who disagreed; "same" = find a matching partner). Mechanism: `buildGroups` (engine/phases/pairing.js) grew a two-term partner score — answer preference (+2, from the source's `byPlayer` map, case-insensitive) outranks repeat-avoidance (+1) — reducing exactly to the old first-non-repeat greedy when no answerOf is given; best-effort by construction, so a lopsided split (5 Cats / 1 Dog) pairs leftover students with each other and NOBODY is benched for lack of an opposite; the final odd player follows `oddHandling` as always; missing source data degrades to random pairing with a loud warn. Validator (server + editor mirror): requires assign:"pairwise", `from` must exist and be a collect-choice, mode enum, conflict with `reusePairsFrom` rejected (reuse dictates the groups), schema `contexts: ['topLevel']` so the wave-1 foreach guard covers it. Composes untouched with rotatePairsFrom/oddHandling and all existing pairs consumers (reveal scope:"pair", vote matchupsFromPairs, reuse/rotate). Simple view shows "partners who answered differently in <step>"; AI guidance gained a worked example (pick → why → pair-swap reveal). End-to-end proof: new `games/_sim-pairby` fixture + `scripts/simulate-pairby.js` (even split: every pair cross-answer; lopsided: exactly one cross pair, all six players seated) — 0 errors live. 1279 tests (18 new: pair-by.test.js).
- 2026-08-26 (phase-interop review, wave 1: the foreach container gets honest): a full producer/consumer review of how the 28 phase types compose (stress case: rounds of student-written would-you-rather questions with opposite-answer pairing) found the weak seam is GROUPING — three grouping systems (pairwise-collect `pairs`, team-split `teams`, merge's internal groups) with no bridges, no way to build groups from what students answered, and a foreach trap: the validator accepted collect's rotation/pairing fields on sub-phases while the runtime remap layer never rewrites them (outputs land under virtual `_fe:` ids nothing can consume) — a config using them silently read no data at game time. Wave 1 ships the guard + the cheap remap wins. (1) **Schema-driven context restriction**: collect's `rotateFrom`/`rotateOffset`/`assign`/`pairsFrom`/`oddHandling`/`rotatePairsFrom`/`reusePairsFrom`/`prefillFromAssigned`/`appendOnly` now carry `contexts: ['topLevel']` in phase-schemas.js; `getFields` filters per-field contexts, so `getAllowedFieldNames` excludes them in foreach and the standard sub-phase allow-list check fires. New `getTopLevelOnlyFieldNames()` powers a specific validator message ("only works on a top-level step, not inside a For Each round") instead of generic unknown-field; `/api/phase-schemas` ships the list as `topLevelOnlyFields` and editor.js mirrors the check in validateConfig (hardcoded fallback list kept). Storyboard/planner prompt told the rule. No existing game or recipe used these in subPhases (scanned). (2) **Foreach remap coverage extended** (foreach-remap.js): sibling refs in `instruction` (ai-process), `correctAnswer` (collect-choice), and string-valued `choices` now remap to virtual ids like message/prompt/input/content always did — previously they silently read the nonexistent original id. Still open from the review (NEXT-STEPS "Phase interop"): answer-keyed grouping (`pairBy`, the missing brick), pairs/teams unification, reveal inside foreach. 1261 tests (16 new: foreach-subphase-guard.test.js + 4 remap cases).
- 2026-08-25 (prod runs DB-less, Good Question promoted to built-in): the owner's /good-question host link showed the activity picker on jamyard.xyz: prod's /api/games returns ZERO user games while local returns 45, and user games are appended only `if (DB_ENABLED)` — **Render has no DATABASE_URL** (render.yaml never declared it; the dashboard either never had it or lost it), so the live site has been on the filesystem fallback: teacher-created activities land on ephemeral disk and die on every deploy (this is why "Questions for Michael" was unrecoverable), and snapshots/feedback/featured-overrides/AI-cap are equally non-durable there. Fixes shipped: (1) `good-question-bad-question` promoted from Neon user game to repo built-in (`games/`, snapshot map entry) — the durable store prod actually reads; kept unfeatured (deep link + vanity URL work regardless; the owner ★ can feature it once the DB is back). (2) render.yaml now declares DATABASE_URL (sync: false) with a loud warning comment. STILL OPEN, owner action: set DATABASE_URL in the Render dashboard (same value as local .env), and delete the now-duplicate `good-question-bad-question` user row from Neon (agent delete was permission-blocked; harmless locally, invisible on prod until the env var lands). 1246 tests.
- 2026-08-25 (preview takeover bug + vanity URLs): two fixes riding the Good Question wave. (1) **Preview was eating its own players**: since the 2026-08-24 join-policy takeover, /prototype pretend students kicked each other out one by one — same-origin iframes in one tab SHARE sessionStorage, so every player iframe read the previous player's `playerToken` and the server treated each join as "same student, new screen" (server log: 8 consecutive "Taking over session via token" in one launch, one seat left). Fix: `IS_PROTOTYPE` guard in player.js — prototype-mode players never read or write the shared `playerToken` key, tokens stay in memory per iframe (in-iframe reconnect still works via `currentToken`; real classrooms untouched). Verified over CDP: 4 players seated with clean joins, question 1 + bot fill green. (2) **Vanity URLs**: new `vanity-urls.json` (slug -> game id) at repo root; server registers each as a redirect to `/host?game=<id>` at startup, refusing malformed or reserved slugs loudly (VANITY_RESERVED mirrors the surface routes). First entry: **jamyard.xyz/good-question** -> good-question-bad-question. New tests: vanity map shape/reserved-collision guard. 1246 tests (3 new).
- 2026-08-25 (no-winners quiz mode, one-shot fix): the owner's "judge these customer interview questions, good or bad, reveal the class split, no winners" idea failed to one-shot in Create. Root cause reproduced with the real planner: "no winners" forbade the quiz brick (the only brick with right answers, and it always appended a leaderboard), so the AI hand-rolled 18 collect-choice/reveal steps — over the 8-step rule, only 8 of the questions, no answer key, and near the 1500-token output cap (truncation there = "The AI reply was not a storyboard", the failure the owner saw). Fix in three parts. (1) **`leaderboard: false` on the quiz brick** (`appendQuizChain` in step-suggestions.js): every question still compiles to a graded collect-choice plus the answer announce with `{{q.barChart}}`, but no standings phase is appended and the teams wiring is skipped; safe because points only ever surface via a leaderboard (collect-choice has no per-player points toast). (2) **Planner prompt taught the mode** (generateStoryboard): quiz brick described as usable "even with no winners" via leaderboard: false; the honesty rule now reads "quiz brick with its leaderboard on"; new rule: teacher-supplied question/statement lists go in ONE quiz step's questions array, never a chain of collect-choice steps, and a requested shuffle means writing the questions array in shuffled order (never grouped by category). (3) **max_tokens 1500 → 3000** (a 15-question quiz storyboard truncated mid-JSON). Re-ran the owner's exact prompt one-shot: 3-step storyboard, compiles to 19 phases, validator clean, no leaderboard, questions interleaved. Also built and saved the activity itself as user game `good-question-bad-question` (10 questions: the owner's 8 plus 2 in the same Mom Test spirit, shuffled; speedBonus false, 20s timer, no leaderboard); robot playtest completed all 22 phases with zero findings. 1243 tests (2 new: no-winners compile shape, prompt-content guard).
- 2026-08-24 (field-test feedback wave: six fixes): owner's testing notes, all shipped same-day. (1) **The plan-intro dialog points at the chat**: a new cyan callout line ("The fastest way to reshape it: tell the Design with AI chat on the right...") sits mid-dialog with a cyan left bar, and closing the dialog pulses the chat panel (inset cyan ring ×3, `chat-spotlight`; inset on purpose — outset shadows die under clip-path) and focuses its input; the seen-key bumped to `lanyardPlanIntroSeen2` so teachers who dismissed v1 see the new line once. (2) **Opening ≠ owning: the yard only gains a copy on the first real edit.** Customize paths where the teacher chose nothing (no AI questions and no knobs; or "Skip, just copy it" with untouched knobs) now stash the config in sessionStorage (`lanyard-pending-copy`) and open `/designer/edit?draft=copy` UNSAVED; the editor's autosave creates the game on the first dirty save via a shared `createNewGame()` (also the Save button's create path; retries 409 id collisions with numeric suffixes — proven live against the owner's real `snowball-my-version` row), then MyGames/Recents/URL flip happen. Touched knobs, answered questions, and the quiz/bluff panels keep the eager save (choosing IS editing). Also cut: `Recents.add(original)` on the bare Customize click (looking must leave no trace; Preview/Host clicks still record recents). E2E-verified over CDP: draft mode in, nothing on the server, one edit → copy exists, draft key cleared. (3) **The projected join line is readable from the back row**: `.join-instructions` was 16px muted gray under a 108px room code; now ~24px ink with the address itself ("jamyard.xyz/player") as a Bricolage 800 `~36px` span (`.join-host`, built with createElement/textContent). (4) **Rank looks draggable now**: the ☰ grip was 1.2rem #aaa (invisible); now 1.45rem muted-brown with a dashed right border, shared by rank AND match rows, plus a rank hint line ("Drag the cards into order, or tap the arrows.", `.match-hint` idiom). (5) **Students can't join twice**: new `engine/join-policy.js` `classifyJoin` (10 unit tests) — a token matching a still-CONNECTED player (duplicated tab shares sessionStorage) is a TAKEOVER: the seat moves to the new socket, the old tab gets new event `session-replaced` ("You joined again on another screen, so this one signed off.", nulls its room state so auto-rejoin can't ping-pong) and is force-disconnected; a typed name already in use by a connected player is REFUSED with a friendly join-error (same student: use the first screen or wait out the ping timeout, then the name-fallback reconnect restores their work; classmates add an initial). Case-insensitive, trimmed; anonymous rooms unaffected (typed names are discarded there). Guide quick-fix row added. Simulator bots got unique names (BOT_NAMES pool is 6, chaos runs 12 — the guard rightly refused the duplicates; caught by chaos dream-vacation dropping to 6 rankers). (6) **"A bit more time" v2 — server-timed phases**: new `engine/phase-timer.js` (`armPhaseTimer`/`extendPhaseTimer`, 8 fake-timer tests): merge, rank, match, sort, rate, checklist, and wager now arm through it (normalizing on rate.js's strong phaseInstanceId staleness guard; handle still lives at `room.phaseState.timer` so every cleanup()/manual-close clearTimeout is untouched), the extend handler re-arms the recorded deadline for `SERVER_TIMED_EXTENDABLE` before broadcasting (no armed timer = press ignored), host screen shows the chip on all 7 (client countdown shift was already generic), console allow-list mirrors. The teachers' rule, now in /guide: the button appears wherever the whole class works against ONE shared countdown; per-turn clocks (relay, turn) and pacing beats (announce, leaderboard) stay out — this also answers "why didn't I see it on Questions for Michael": its merge step has it now. simulate-extend-timer step 5 INVERTED (merge press now must reach every player) — 12 checks green; full chaos suite 6/6 games 0 errors 0 warnings; simulate-snowball clean. 1241 tests (18 new).
- 2026-08-23 (reconnect grace, phone gate, fullscreen chip): three owner asks. (1) **Player reconnect grace 30s → 3 min** (`PLAYER_GRACE_MS`, named next to `HOST_GRACE_MS`): a dropped student keeps their identity (scores, team, submissions attribution) for a school-wifi-sized window; known trade-off, accepted: a gone-for-good student blocks "everyone's in" auto-advance until removed, but the teacher's close button always overrides. (2) **Editor phone gate**: under 700px, /designer/edit shows a full-cover Totem card ("This editor needs a bigger screen", back-to-library door) instead of the three-column soup; pure CSS (`#mobile-gate` in editor.css), the editor stays loaded underneath so widening a window or rotating a tablet uncovers it. Screenshot-verified at 650px (gate) and 1280px (untouched); headless phone emulation lies about viewport size (laid out 762px while claiming 390), the computed styles were the proof. (3) **Full screen chip on the host screen**: a paper chip under the Sound toggle (`#fullscreen-toggle`, shares `.sfx-toggle` styling), toggles `requestFullscreen` on the document, label kept truthful via `fullscreenchange` (Esc/F11 included), hidden where the API is missing; added to regen-carousel-shots' chrome-hide list. Also investigated "I'm not seeing A bit more time": the button works locally and on jamyard.xyz (CDP-probed a live snowball collect: chip present under the 1:28 countdown) — it only exists while a countdown is actually running on a collect/choice/vote/estimate step, so timerless steps and announce/reveal screens never show it (by design; the close button is the pacing tool there). 1223 tests.
- 2026-08-23 (anonymous mode, every activity): the editor's Activity Settings gains a universal "Student names" choice — "Students type their name" (default) or "Anonymous play names". Stored as top-level `anonymous: true` (absent = collect; validator rejects non-boolean loudly so a mistyped value can't silently collect names). In an anonymous room the join handler discards whatever the client sent, unread, and assigns a "Color Animal" play name (`engine/anonymous-names.js`, ~570 combos, all within the 20-char registry cap, numbered Player fallback); play names flow through every downstream surface (roster, console, leaderboards, reconnect id-migration) for free because they ARE the player name. Player screen: new public `GET /api/rooms/:code/info` ({code, game, anonymous} — projector-public facts only) lets the join form hide the "Your name" box the moment 4 letters are typed and show "You'll get a play name when you join" instead; the lookup is UI courtesy only — if it fails, the server still enforces anonymity. `join-success` now carries `anonymous`. The revise/design-chat pipeline preserves the flag via `carryAnonymousFlag` next to `carryRecipeStamp` (the AI rebuilds whole configs and doesn't know the field). Smoke-verified live: typed "Max Real Name" became "Onyx Yak", empty-name join got "Navy Moose", control room still honored "Jordan". Closes the "anonymous mode" item from the safety not-yet list. 1223 tests (9 new).

- 2026-08-22 (the "Human or AI" one-shot fails honestly now, and the error check stops shouting): owner tried to create "Human or AI" on the Create page and got a hollow game — the matcher only knew recipes, so the idea fell to the storyboard, whose bricks can't generate AI-written rival answers; the storyboard AI faked the premise with words (announce text promising "one is from AI", a choice list literally reading "dynamic", a collect field labeled "Leave blank", reveals showing nothing) — while built-in `human-vs-ai-birthday-party-battle` IS that exact activity. Three changes. (1) **The matcher knows the ready-made activities**: unforced `matchRecipe` calls now carry the built-in catalog (id/name/description/playTime; forced refits excluded on purpose) and may answer `{"game": id}` when a finished activity already IS the idea (same mechanic, not merely same topic); the service sanitizes it like the recipe shape (alternates stay recipes), the from-description route resolves the id against real built-ins (invented ids fall to noMatch) and ships `{existingGame, explanation, alternates}`; the Create page renders a "Good news: this already exists" view with Host / Preview / See-it-in-the-Library doors, a pointer at the library's Customize, and the recipe alternates as "Or build your own version" refit cards (refitToAlternate's carry-over now tolerates a game top pick). Live-verified: the Human or AI idea now returns the built-in with a clean explanation; a poll idea still compiles Class Poll. (2) **The storyboard stops promising what bricks can't do**: generateStoryboard's rules now spell out what no brick provides (AI-written content during play, side-by-side pairs, pairing, elimination, branching) and forbid step text promising any of it; when the idea's HEART needs a missing mechanic the AI returns `{"cantBuild": true, "reason"}` instead of a hollow lookalike — served as 200 (not an error), rendered in the plan dialog as "This one needs a trick we don't have yet" + reason + Pick-from-Recipes door. Live-verified: the same idea now yields cantBuild with a plain-language reason. (3) **"Check for Errors" became "Test with Robots" and the panel stopped being an essay** (owner: "clunky, overwhelming, a whole bunch of writing"): DEEP_REVIEW_EXTRA (fun/pacing/engagement coaching) is CUT from the deep review — that's the Ask AI chat's job, with applyable cards — so deep review = the error checklist on Sonnet + the robot playtest; the panel (now "Playtest Report") drops the AI prose summary, keeps the one-line playtest verdict, shows only error/warning findings capped at 6 ("N more notes are marked on the step cards"; suggestion-grade notes still badge the cards via the silent on-save light review). 1214 tests (10 new). **Later same session, owner's follow-up ("still too complicated, the user shouldn't see this, Apply Fix leads to work that doesn't make sense"): the findings list is gone entirely and so is the whole per-issue fix pipeline.** The panel is now a VERDICT + ONE DOOR: one line ("4 robot players played your activity start to finish... Nothing broken!" / "reached the end, but N things need fixing") and a single "Have AI fix this" button that hands the findings to the design chat as a plain user message (`sendDesignChat(text)`, new programmatic entry in chat-panel.js; returns false when busy) — the chat proposes ONE validated change with Apply/Revert, the pipeline teachers already know. Deleted outright: the panel's issue list, requestFix / showFixPreview (diff modal) / dismissIssue / renderFieldDiff in editor.js, the per-step "✨ Apply Fix" buttons in renderAISuggestions (suggestion text stays, quiet), `POST /api/games/fix-issue`, `AIService.fixIssue` + mock/real variants, and all their CSS (.review-fix-btn, .fix-preview-modal, .fix-diff*, .review-issue*). The chat hand-off message caps at 6 findings with phase names humanized. Verified: endpoint 404s, suite green.
- 2026-08-22 (storyboard joins Totem + no Q in room codes): two owner reports. (1) **"Plan it step by step" was jammed against the left edge, unstyled**: showStoryboardFlow used `picker-overlay`/`picker-modal`, classes that live only in editor.css, which /designer never loads, so the overlay rendered in-flow with no positioning at all. Switched to the page's `template-picker-overlay`/`-modal` (centered fixed flex, Totem gesso) and moved every inline paste-up-era style (white cards, 2px black borders, Nunito) to a new `.sb-*` section in designer/styles.css: paper step cards with Bricolage caps heads, recipe-field plank inputs for name/step text, dashed-rule quiz question rows, paper ✕ chips, vermillion problems line. Screenshot-verified with a seeded storyboard via a CDP evaluate (scratchpad shot-storyboard.mjs, screenshot.js's mechanics + Runtime.evaluate to open the modal, since it normally only appears after a no-match). (2) **Room codes never contain Q**: students read a projected Q as O and mistyped the join code; Q is dropped from the generateCode alphabet (codes are letters-only, so O itself stays unambiguous), test-pinned. 1204 tests.

- 2026-08-22 (Create page: chips submit + matcher alternates): owner's observation that "I want my class to laugh together" only ever surfaces One Voice, when Doodle Bluff / Trivia Bluff / Creative Vote fit too. Three changes. (1) **"Try one of these" chips now launch the match directly** (clicking one is the decision; the sentence still lands in the idea box) instead of just filling the box and waiting for a second Make-it press. (2) **Chip set rebalanced for goal spread**: the elimination-game chip is cut; added anonymous-feedback, encouragement-wall, bluffing-game, and get-to-know-each-other chips (7 total, each reliably matching a strong recipe). (3) **The recipe matcher returns up to 2 alternates**: matchRecipe's prompt asks for `alternates: [{recipe, why}]` on goal-shaped ideas (empty on specific ones; sanitized in the service, capped at 2, self-echo dropped), the from-description route resolves the ids against real recipes (unknown ids drop silently) and ships `{id, name, icon, description, why}` cards, and the match preview renders them under "Or maybe one of these instead:". Clicking an alternate re-POSTs the same idea with a new `recipeId` body field, which narrows the matcher to that one recipe so AI only fills its params; the outgoing pick and unchosen alternates carry over client-side as the next view's alternates so flipping between candidates never dead-ends. Live-verified: laugh-together now returns One Voice + Doodle Bluff + Trivia Bluff, and the forced doodle-bluff refit fills phrasePrompt/rounds/drawTimer. Same day, owner's follow-up: **the "Start from scratch" blank-editor link is removed** from the Create page (the advanced line is just "Prefer to build it yourself? Browse recipes" now) along with the home tooltips that advertised it; /designer/edit with no game still works if typed, but no surface links there. Later same day, after the owner hit a forced-refit refusal live ("no recipe designed specifically for students to get to know each other" when clicking an alternate card): **forced mode can no longer refuse** — with `recipeId` set, matchRecipe gets a forced prompt variant that says the recipe is already chosen, fill parameters only (defaults or classroom-safe inventions when the idea gives nothing), and the noMatch shape isn't offered at all; the refit error message also lost its double period. An agent then swept all 7 chips + every alternate refit against the live server (14 real Haiku calls): zero dead ends, every refit filled. Its soft-spot report led to one more owner call: **Doodle Bluff is the primary "laugh together" match now**, via a matcher-prompt tie-breaker (laughter-shaped ideas prefer comedy from student creations; quieter cooperative games become alternates) — verified 3/3 live runs return doodle-bluff primary with trivia-bluff + one-voice as alternates. Gotcha confirmed: the no-em-dash style test scans AI prompt strings in services/ too. And a polish pass on the "Here's what I'd set up" preview (owner screenshot showed TIER1PROMPTS labels + comma-mashed prompt lists): the response now carries `paramLabels` (the recipe's own parameter labels; client humanizes raw ids as fallback), array params render one item per line with dashed separators, and the label column widened 120px → 160px. 1203 tests (6 new).
- 2026-08-21 (merge pen): the shared merge draft is now one-writer-at-a-time. Owner hit the v1 failure live ("Questions for Michael", a Snowball variant): whole-text last-write-wins let two students typing within a debounce+RTT of each other silently destroy each other's sentences. Now each group has ONE PEN: writing claims it (first accepted merge-draft), agreeing releases it (so a partner can refine with no wait), and it goes stale after `MERGE_PEN_IDLE_MS` (2500ms, server.js) so the partner's "Take the pen" button lights up; enforcement is lazy (penHolder/penAt on the group, no per-group timers). A write that races a fresh pen is rejected and the racer's box is snapped back to the shared truth plus told who holds it. Player UI: read-only box with `.merge-locked` styling, "[Name] is writing…" button that becomes "Take the pen" after the same idle window client-side (an early tap is just denied with the truth), status line "Talk it out while they type", pen handoff focuses the box. Courtesy fix that ships with it: the draft debounce flushes on blur so a quick Agree never loses the final word. New events merge-take-pen / merge-pen (+ schema); MERGE_START/reconnect carry pen state; deliberately NOT OT/CRDT (the pedagogy wants one scribe and a conversation; spec §3.4 updated). New scripts/simulate-merge-pen.js (15 checks: claim, raced-write snapback, fresh deny, idle steal, release-on-agree, full agree flow); simulate-snowball unbroken BECAUSE agree releases the pen (its write-agree-partner-edits flow needs no idle wait). 1197 tests.
- 2026-08-21 (carousel activity shots): the homepage carousel card now pins a real projector shot beside the text (text pushes left, 246x185 paper print right, name chip drops to 21px; owner picked this "half-card screenshot" treatment from a three-way mockup study incl. a mini-totem alternative). Shots live in `screens/home/shots/<gameId>.png` served at `/home-shots/`, made by **scripts/regen-carousel-shots.js** (server running): drives the real host screen over CDP per featured built-in with 4 bots (2 submit answers so the pile looks alive), advances to the first input phase (per-game overrides: closer clicks:2 lands on "Window seat", trivia-bluff clicks:0 stops before its AI call), hides teacher chrome, captures at 900x675 so the page's own responsive layout composes the thumbnail (NO cropping, that approach kept cutting text). RUN IT after any visual redesign or featured-set change, commit the PNGs (12 shots, ~560KB). Missing shot = text-only card automatically (user creations never get shots). Client remembers per-id hit/miss so rotation never re-probes; late image loads only apply if the carousel is still on that activity. GOTCHA re-hit: no-em-dash test scans CSS comments inside HTML files. 1197 tests.
- 2026-08-21 (totem hover fix): owner report: pushing the mouse vertically through a stack seam made TWO slabs drift in the same direction. Two causes, both fixed in shared/totem.css: (1) the transform transition lived on the base `.t-block-btn` state, so crossing a seam animated the departing block (returning to 0) and the arriving block (sliding out) at once, and with alternating slide signs both translate the same visual way; the transition now rides only on the hovered/focused state, so the arriving block glides and the departing one snaps home. (2) rotated blocks poke corners past their row into the neighbor's strip and stole the pointer there, so the handoff point wandered mid-seam; `.t-row` is now `position:relative; z-index:0` (per-row stacking contexts) so the later row wins its strip outright, hit-testing only, paint order unchanged. Verified with a real-mouse CDP probe (scratchpad hover-probe.js recipe: Input.dispatchMouseEvent sweeps across every seam, counting displaced blocks): home totem 4 rows + editor sv-stack 16 rows, every probe point slid exactly one block, handoffs monotonic. NOTE for future testing: synthetic JS mouse events do NOT drive CSS :hover; use CDP Input.dispatchMouseEvent.
- 2026-08-21 ("A bit more time" v1): teachers can add 30 seconds to a running input timer, from the projector (a paper chip under the countdown) or the teacher console (button in the live controls). Mechanism: `extend-timer` (host/console -> server, teacher-gated via isTeacherSocket, stale/kind/closed-guarded, allow-list `EXTENDABLE_TIMER_PHASES` = collect / collect-choice / vote / estimate, the phases whose countdown authority is the HOST SCREEN clicking its own close button at 0) -> server broadcasts `timer-extended {addSeconds:30}` to the room AND the teachers channel -> every screen shifts its local countdown (load-bearing on player screens: their clock auto-submits at 0, so a projector-only stretch would cut students off early). Server-armed timers (announce, merge, rank, match, sort, rate, checklist, wager, leaderboard) are EXCLUDED until they move to a re-armable shared timer (that's v2); `turn` stays excluded on purpose (its clock is a fairness mechanic). teacher-phase + console snapshot now carry `timer`; host/player startTimer state lifted to module level (timerRemaining/timerTotal). New: scripts/simulate-extend-timer.js (11 checks incl. teacher-only gate + post-close rejection), event-schema entry + tests, /guide live-controls line. Screenshot-verified on the host screen (two presses: 1:23 -> 2:23). 1197 tests.

Featured USER games now populate publicly: game-visibility.js previously ignored `featured` on source:"user" rows (only myIds showed), which is why owner-starred user creations never appeared for anyone else; now user games show when mine OR featured (server already stored the flag in the user config row). Insight that shaped it: options 1 and 2 are the SAME compiled game (a prepared question list in the params; the only difference is who typed the list), so the recipe has two compile branches, not three. (1) **Compiler `$else`**: the `$if`/`$value` conditional grew an either/or form `{"$if": c, "$value": v, "$else": w}` (works on whole nodes too) — needed because a branching `next` can't be expressed by field-dropping alone; plus object-item sub-fields now fill from their declared `default` during coercion (form UIs omit blank optional fields; templates reading `${item.field}` need the key). (2) **Recipe trivia-bluff v-same**: new `questionSource` enum (live|prepared, default live) + `questions` array param ({question with ___, truth, houseLie optional, max 10}); a second `$repeat-prepared` block compiles qshow→qlies→qvote→qreveal per fact (same scoring: 100 truth, 50 foolPoints, decoy as optional choicePool literal that silently drops when empty), `intro.next` and the scoreboard `from` branch via `$else`; live mode compiles BYTE-IDENTICAL to before (asserted during regen), so the shipped built-in changed only its stamp (now carries questionSource:"live", questions:[]; version stays "1" so existing copies' knobs keep working). (3) **New `setupPanel: "bluff"`** (schema now validates panels from a SETUP_PANELS set): the library Customize dialog for Trivia Bluff opens with three doors — "AI picks facts during the game" (rounds + lie-timer knobs), "AI writes the facts now" (topic + count → `POST /api/games/bluff-facts` → `AIService.generateBluffFacts`, Sonnet because wrong facts on a projector are the failure mode, mock-mode canned facts keep it keyless-testable → `engine/bluff-questions.js` `cleanBluffQuestions`: blank required as ___, truth required, truth-matching decoy cleared not dropped), and "I write my own facts" — both prepared doors share one always-editable fact list (question/truth/decoy per card) behind the same review-before-save gate as the quiz panel, validated client-side by `SetupKnobs.validateBluffList`. Verified: live-server smoke of the prepared compile (qshow1…qreveal2 chain + summed scoreboard) and the summary's setupPanel; full game-loader validation of a prepared compile is in the drift-guard suite. 1194 tests (28 new).
- 2026-08-16 (evening — the ninja-turtle incident: revise JSON repair + loud save failures + Preview save guard): owner field-tested someones-got-you (Customize "didn't really work", then the design chat themed it but "in preview it didn't really work"); full forensic reproduced both. **Bug 1**: the config's typographic “{{notes.assigned}}” comes back from Sonnet normalized to UNESCAPED straight quotes inside a JSON string → parse error → /api/games/revise 500s → Customize fell back to a plain un-themed copy (raw model output captured to prove it; near-deterministic for any config with curly quotes — six games + three recipes carry them as house style). A STRICT JSON RULE in GAME_GENERATOR_PROMPT did NOT stop the normalization (verified empirically), so the durable fix is `repairJsonStringQuotes` (exported, ai-service.js): inside a string, a quote whose next non-whitespace char can't legally follow a string end gets escaped — wired as a LAST-RESORT parse fallback in reviseGame only after normal parsing fails, so healthy responses are untouched. The failing ninja revise now succeeds end to end (validated, robot-playtested clean); curly quotes stay (voice preserved). **Bug 2**: the chat's applied theme was never saved anywhere — the user was editing the BUILT-IN (Customize had stranded them), the PUT 401s without the owner password, autosave failures were console.warn-only, and testGame's "only open if save succeeded" guard was really just `if (gameId)` (always true for existing games) so Preview opened the prototype showing the server's stale original. Fixed: `lastSaveError` drives a coral "Save failed" header state + one toast per failure streak (autosave and saveGame both feed it), and Preview now refuses to open until a save lands (isDirty check after saveGame, the Host button's existing pattern). 1169 tests (3 new).

Two-tier cost model via one stateless endpoint `POST /api/games/chat`: a single Haiku triage-and-answer call (`AIService.designChat`, config sent as a compact plain-text summary via new exported `summarizeConfigForChat`) both replies and classifies the turn; only a clear concrete request chains into the existing Sonnet `reviseGame` pipeline server-side, then gets `carryRecipeStamp` + `validate` exactly like /revise, and returns as `{kind:'proposal', reply, proposal:{updatedConfig, summary, structural}}`. History is client-held per page load (proposal turns enter it as prose with a status token — pending/applied/discarded/reverted — never as configs; capped client-side and re-trimmed server-side by exported `trimChatHistory`). Hardening the modal never had: **Apply is disabled while the proposal has structural errors** (the library Customize flow's stricter policy, now shared), **Revert = one-step undo** (config snapshot at apply time, so interim inline edits are recoverable too; a new Apply replaces the snapshot), and the apply path finally calls `renderSettings()` (the modal forgot it, so AI name/description changes never refreshed the left sidebar). Entry points: the Simple-view per-step button and the Builder-rail step button (both jump to Simple with a "Talking about: step" context chip that rides one send); the header ✨ Ask AI button is REMOVED same-day (owner's call: the panel is always open in Simple view, the button was redundant). Panel is Simple-view-only (hidden in Builder/Advanced via DOM-truth sync), in-flow paste-up column (paper bg, vermillion user bubbles, gesso proposal scraps), all message DOM createElement/textContent (registered in xss-sinks FILES). teacher-profile.js now loads in the editor so the chat knows the class.
